- **R-STOP.** A "reading follows" test asserts the words are in `text_units`, never only that a reading exists.
- **R-CLOCK.** Workerd freezes `Date.now()` during synchronous work, so no timing taken inside the object sees compute. A test asserts bytes pulled, calls made and ledger rows, never a compute time from the object's clock; a wall time is taken by the test itself outside the object and only where this plan says so.
- **R-PROXY** (K4733, new): the copy is reached through the environment's proxy only, unchanged (Chromium `proxy: {server: HTTPS_PROXY}`, curl and node as configured). The direct route (`--noproxy`, unset proxy variables, a private trust store) is refused by auto mode and never tried. The proxy's own share is read beside `Server-Timing` and is not the product's: 1.0-4.0 s to first byte on a new connection (about half of them), 0.9-1.4 s a call in the browser, about 8 MB/s on an upload (`diagnosis-R7-screens.md`, "The proxy's share, measured").
- **R-QUIET** (new): the PERFORMANCE re-time runs after this round's redeploy on a quiet copy (no walker's bulk upload and no reading tick of a large file in the same object); a time taken while a tick runs is reported as that, not as a screen's time.

### G59 · A case citing a vouched upload cannot be signed: the history checker looks for a dotless snapshot where it is not (B G9/G29 vouched; diagnosis-R7-blockers.md B1)

**Rows.**
> **B.md:13** · G9/G29 (vouched upload) | the same flow with the upload VOUCHED first ("Vouch for this copy") | 1280 | admin | new | twice: INFO-2026-0144 in "Walk T51 rewalk6 B" (CASE-2026-8469 minted, edition 1 prepared) and INFO-2026-0166 in "Walk T51 rewalk6 B vouched"; each: Sign and publish now > Confirm > passphrase > REAUTH_DUE > password > Go on | NEW (BLOCKER: a case citing a file its member has vouched for can never be signed, and the screen names no cause) | `op=caseratify` answers 409 `GATE_REFUSED`, finding `C-12.2` "snapshot '_history/snapshots/upload-<document sha>_<time of the vouch> ...' recorded in manifest entry '<time>_<id>' is missing" (repairs: "record a history-loss finding and re-snapshot current state"), `member` the uploaded document. The missing snapshot is the one made at the moment of the vouch (11:27:51Z, 11:52:03Z). The screen says only "This cannot be signed yet because it did not pass Civicsmith's checks before publication. Fix the problems listed, then sign again. Nothing was written." and lists no problem. Reproduced 2 of 2 vouched; 1 of 1 unvouched signs. "Check before publishing" (`publishpreflight`) passes before the refusal, so a member learns only at the last step | shots/B/sign-refused-1280.png
Owner **record-grammar**; *prov* none; the generated `case-checker/program.mjs` embeds the same code and is regenerated by BOB at the round end (sha changes; no case-checker job). Seam: control-plane's `t51-round7-seam.test.mjs`.
(1) **Cause, certain.** The writer names an upload's file `snapshots/upload-${sha}` with no extension (`capture/index.mjs:842`). The vouch snapshots it into the history under the vouch's key, and the image builder stores it correctly (`record-core/index.mjs:723-731` `RecordCore.snapPath`, dotless branch `_history/<dir><name>_<key>`; also `promotion/history.mjs:95-98`, `screens-inquiry/document.mjs:754`). The checker builds the path with an extension-only formula: `record-grammar/bundle.mjs:588-590`, `dot = name.lastIndexOf('.')` is -1 for a dotless name, so `name.slice(0, -1)` drops the last character of the sha and `name.slice(-1)` puts it AFTER the key; it looks for `_history/snapshots/upload-<sha minus last char>_<key><last char>`, which does not exist, and finding C-12.2 "snapshot '...' recorded in manifest entry '<key>' is missing" (:601) results. Second site, same family: `bundle.mjs:653` maps a history file back to its manifest entry with `/_((?:\d{8}T\d{6}Z)_[0-9a-f]{8})\./`, which needs a dot after the key, so the correctly stored dotless copy is also reported "history file ... maps to no manifest entry". The gate reads the findings of each cited member at `ratification/index.mjs:1444` and refuses `GATE_REFUSED` (`ratification/ops.mjs:541`, carrying `findings`). Reproduced offline (`diagR7a/c122.mjs`, recorded in the diagnosis): the walker's message exactly, plus the orphan finding. An unvouched upload has no promotion that snapshots it, so it signs (the walker's 1 of 1).
(2) **Missed:** every history fixture uses names with extensions (`bundle.md`, `data/x.json`: `test/m/record-grammar/fixtures/bundles.mjs:166-181`, "C-12 manifest accounting", "elided snapshots"), and the checker's `expected.json` was recorded from the old catalogue over them; no test vouches an upload and then runs the gate (the seam tests never sign a case; `test/m/ratification` signs documents with extension-bearing history); the only upload shape walked before was the unvouched one (G9/G29).
(3) **Failing-first** (red now). (a) `bio-plane/test/m/record-grammar/bundle.test.mjs` plus a fixture in `fixtures/bundles.mjs`: a bundle whose manifest entry snapshots a dotless name (`snapshots/upload-<sha>`), one in a subfolder (`a/b/c`) AND one in a DOTTED directory with a dotless file (`a.b/c`: `bundle.mjs:589` takes `lastIndexOf('.')` over the whole path while `RecordCore.snapPath` takes it over the name after the last `/`, so only this case tells the two apart), with the copies at `RecordCore.snapPath`'s paths; asserts zero C-12.2 findings (no "is missing", no "maps to no manifest entry"). (a2) a PARITY test in a module above both, `bio-plane/test/m/ratification/r7-snappath-parity.test.mjs` (record-grammar, layer 1, cannot import record-core, so this is the fourth copy of the derivation after `snapPath`, `promotion/history.mjs:95-98` and `screens-inquiry/document.mjs:754`): the helper's path equals `RecordCore.snapPath` for dotted, dotless, subfolder and dotted-directory names, so the family cannot drift again. (b) The seam file, the walker's case through the real door: a member uploads a 356 B text file (`captureupload`), files it (`uploadAndFile`), vouches (`release` with the acknowledgment the screen sends), cites a sentence of it in a question, concludes, prepares a case (`publish`), registers a key (`signerregister`), and signs (`caseratify` with the sitting from `casedocument`); asserts 200, the case published, `publishedcase` `findings: 1`, `complete: true`. Control in the same file: the unvouched upload signs. Red now with 409 GATE_REFUSED C-12.2. Providers: record-grammar (and the regenerated case-checker, which BOB builds before the door test is run whole).
(4) Platform check **N** for the logic (pure path arithmetic; it ran identically in node); **P** at the re-walk: the walker's vouch-then-sign repeated on biosmoke8.
**Fix shape.** One helper in `bundle.mjs` that derives a snapshot copy's path exactly as `RecordCore.snapPath` does, used at :590, and the reverse mapping at :653 accepting a key at the end of the name or before the extension. **Old records:** none to repair: the snapshots are stored at the right paths; only the checker looked in the wrong place; no case with a vouched upload was ever signed, so no published case file carries the old embedded checker over such history. CASE-2026-8469 (INFO-2026-0144) and the INFO-2026-0166 draft sign unchanged after the fix.

### G61 · After a money ingest the member's whole Queue refuses `NO_SUCH_KIND` ('ingest-done'): the producers mint three kinds the queue's catalogue does not name (B G55 notice; diagnosis-R7-blockers.md B2)

**Rows.**
> **B.md:22** · G55 (notice) | the notice arrives within the ticks | 1280 | member | new | opened the member's Queue after the ingest finished | NEW (BLOCKER for that member's Queue screen) | the member's whole Queue now refuses: screen "Your list could not be assembled: something on it is described in a word this record does not know, so there is no sentence to show you in place of it. Rather than showing you a line you could not read, the list refuses whole. Nothing has been lost." `op=queue` answers 400 `NO_SUCH_KIND` (C-31.2) "'ingest-done' is not a kind this record's catalogue names. The vocabulary is queuestate.mjs's three lists and nothing else". Also `op=queue&count=true` 400. So the notice the ingest sends is a kind the Queue does not know, and the member who ran an ingest loses the whole Queue (every other item too) until the notice is gone. The administrator's Queue is unaffected (200). Member Queue loads 11.3 s. This state is still in force for the test member at 12:15Z: walker A and BOB will see it | shots/B/queue-member-1280.png
Owner **queue**; *prov* notice-producers (existing tables, unchanged); seam control-plane.
(1) **Cause, certain.** `ingest-done` and `ingest-stopped` are in `notice-producers`' kind table (`notice-producers/index.mjs:161-164` `NOTICE_KINDS`) and minted by `#ingestNotices` (:1457-1498, `FINDING::ingest-<state>::<ingest>`, class FINDING), but the queue's own vocabulary (`queuestate.mjs`: `QUEUE_FINDING_KINDS` :202-391 and the two other lists) does not name them. The mint check refuses the whole answer for a kind the catalogue does not name: `queue/index.mjs:1257-1258` (`classOfKind(it.kind) === null` gives `NO_SUCH_KIND`; `classOfKind` is `queuestate.mjs:428-434`). Reproduced (`diagR7a/chk.mjs`, over `NOTICE_KINDS` against `classOfKind`): `ingest-done`, `ingest-stopped` AND `fetch-done` answer null. The administrator differs because `calculations.ingestNotices({viewer})` answers only the viewer's own ingests (`notice-producers/index.mjs:1457-1466`); the member who ran the ingest gets the item and the whole feed refuses; `op=queue&count=true` goes through the same mint. It stays until the notice leaves the 90-day window (`INGEST_NOTICE_DAYS`). Latent twin: `fetch-done` (G2/R27, a large capture's finished fetch, `notice-producers/index.mjs:161`, item :1432) has the same hole, so the opener of any large capture would lose their Queue the same way; the G2 seam test's assertion (`t51-round6-seam.test.mjs:260`, `queue.includes(name) || queue.includes(BIG_SHA)`) searches the JSON of whatever `op=queue` answered, a refusal included.
(2) **Missed:** `notice-producers/g55-ingest.test.mjs` and `fetch.test.mjs` assert the producer's own table (`NOTICE_KINDS[kind] === "FINDING"`) and the items, never that the QUEUE's catalogue names the kind; `queue/catalogue.test.mjs` checks the catalogue's own lists, not the producers'; `read.test.mjs:101` compares item class with `NOTICE_KINDS`, which agrees with itself; no test mints the feed for a member with a finished ingest.
(3) **Failing-first.** (a) The catalogue cross-check, `bio-plane/test/m/queue/catalogue.test.mjs`: every key of `NOTICE_KINDS` and of `queue-producers`' kind table has `classOfKind(kind) === class` (red now with exactly three names). (b) The seam file: a member is enrolled, uploads an 18,098-row comma CSV (header line equal to the declared header), `contentmint`, `tabledeclare`, `bindingadopt`, `moneyingest {rows: "all", reason}`; the test waits (its own clock, outside the object) until `moneyingestread` says `done`; then `op=queue` and `op=queue&count=true` as that member answer 200 and the feed holds `FINDING::ingest-done::<ingest>`; the administrator's queue is 200 without it. Red now: 400 NO_SUCH_KIND. Same file: a large capture's finished fetch, run AS AN ENROLLED MEMBER (a founder run is green for the wrong reason: A.md:15/43 below), gives the opener a 200 queue with `fetch-done`, and the G2 assertion is tightened to check `ok` and the item's kind. Providers: queue (G61), notice-producers, calculations.
(4) Platform check **N** for the cause (catalogue membership); the door test is L-shaped (Miniflare); **P** at the re-walk: the test member's Queue answers 200 and holds `ingest-done` for the existing ingest a33598cb....
**Fix shape.** Add `ingest-done`, `ingest-stopped` and `fetch-done` to `QUEUE_FINDING_KINDS` with a one-line meaning each (code-origin stand-ins; the UX rows owed, section 5). The queue's refuse-whole doctrine (PL-15, `queue/index.mjs:1200-1256`) is NOT changed here (section 6). **Old records:** none: items are derived on read from `calc_ingests`; once the kind is named, the test member's existing ingest mints its item and the Queue returns. **A.md:15 and A.md:43 placed:** walker A (the founder) opened a large capture that finished and found no "fetched" notice and no `fetch` row; `notice-producers/index.mjs:1410` `#fetchDone` returns nothing when `#active(me)` is false, which probably explains the founder (the bare `admin` against `member:admin`, the family of G58), but that is unproven: it is not a member's loss and not a cause here; the re-walk reads the notice as an enrolled member (**P**) and records the founder's answer beside it.

### G62 · The Sign step says the team's time zone isn't set while Settings shows Pacific Time (B G11 no zone; diagnosis-R7-blockers.md B3). Cause unknown in the live object; certain defects in what the screen reports

**Rows.**
> **B.md:15** · G11 (team with no zone) | Sign section | 1280 | admin | new | read | FIXED for the no-zone side; NEW (MAJOR wording/data) | the ceremony says "Your team's time zone isn't set, and a publishing time is counted in it, so only "Sign and publish now" is offered. An administrator can set it under Settings, Your team." No empty brackets, no second button (rewalk5: "time zone ()" and a second button). BUT Settings > Your team > TIME ZONE reads "Pacific Time (Los Angeles), from City of Oakland and Alameda County. Set when your team's Civicsmith was set up": the ceremony says not set while the settings page says set, so a member cannot schedule a signing and is told to fix something already set. A team with a zone: not exercised (only one team) | shots/B/team-1280.png
Owner **screens-publish (S)**; *prov* ratification (the pre-flight's reason), retrieval (the zone's source); seam control-plane.
(1) **Cause unknown** for why the live pre-flight offers no zone; `diagnosis-R7-blockers.md` records the reproduction tried and not reproduced: with the real profile `oakland-alameda` as `jurisdiction_profiles`, `retrieval.zone()` answers `America/Los_Angeles` (`diagR7a/zone.test.mjs`) and `case-authoring` `publishPreflight` answers `publish_at {offered:true, zone:"America/Los_Angeles"}` both before and after the edition is prepared (`diagR7a/prep.test.mjs`). **Certain:** the sentence is drawn for three different server answers alike: `screens-publish/ceremony.mjs:348-353` (`timed = pre.state==="ok" && pa.offered !== false && !!zone`, else `refusal.PUBLISH_AT_NO_ZONE`) folds (i) no zone held, (ii) `publish_at: null` (the ratification check "not reached", `case-authoring/index.mjs:1904-1906`, whose `publish_at_stated` gives the real reason and which the screen never reads), and (iii) an offer with an empty zone; and its wording "isn't set ... An administrator can set it under Settings" is wrong whenever the cause is not an unset zone. The server's zone: `ratification/index.mjs:676-686` `#publishAtOffer` calls `retrieval.zone()` (`retrieval/index.mjs:486-518`: reads `jurisdiction_profiles`, then local-facts' `<profile>/time_zone`, then the combined profiles); Settings reads the same record through `factstatus` (`screens-settings/group.mjs:152`). Candidates for the live difference: (i) `ratify.reached` false live (`caseRatifyPreflight` unreadable or throwing), so `publish_at` is null; (ii) `retrieval.zone()` answers null live because the `jurisdiction_profiles` setting is unreadable or not the list Settings draws from (the fall-through to the combined profiles is already in the code: `retrieval/index.mjs:183, 202, 515` default `combine` to `combineProfiles`, and `zone()` uses it unless two profiles disagree, so a different local-facts instance at `:497-499` alone cannot make it null); (iii) a stale `publishpreflight` answer. Rewalk 5 saw "time zone ()" with the second button, an offer with an empty zone, which fits (i) or (ii) and not the fixture.
(2) **Missed:** the fixtures steer the zone: `case-authoring/signing.test.mjs:29-46` and the ratification tests set `jurisdiction_profiles` on a record-core fixture and assert the server's answer; `retrieval/t33.test.mjs:171-193` stubs `localFacts`; no test runs the real plane's wiring with a set profile and reads the ceremony's own `publishpreflight`; the screen test uses a canned `publish_at` (G11's tests set `offered` by hand), so the three cases were never told apart.
(3) **Failing-first.** (a) screens-publish `r7-no-zone.test.mjs`: the Sign step drawn from three real pre-flight answers made by the real op (no zone; not reached with `publish_at_stated`; offer with an empty zone) draws the date and time fields only for an offer with a zone, and for the others a sentence naming which of the three it is, never "isn't set" for the second and third (red: one sentence for all). (b) the seam file: Miniflare plane, `jurisdiction_profiles` set to `["oakland-alameda"]` the way setup does, a draft and prepared edition as the member's own acts, then `publishpreflight` (the ceremony's read): `publish_at` is `{offered: true, zone: "America/Los_Angeles"}` and the Sign step drawn from it shows the date and time fields. If (b) is green at the platform's shape the cause is live-only, which is what the logging is for. (c) ratification: the pre-flight's `publish_at` carries `source` (`local-facts` | `profiles` | none) and, when not offered, the reason (`publish_at_stated` or `PUBLISH_AT_NO_ZONE`); retrieval: `zone({why: true})` answers `{zone, source, why}` and the plain `zone()` is unchanged.
(4) Platform check **P** and **L**. **L:** (b) in workerd. **P** after the redeploy: BOB reads, on biosmoke8, `publishpreflight` for the walker's draft (the sign step's `publish_at`, `source`, `publish_at_stated`) and `reviewcopy` (top-level `zone`, `review/index.mjs:874-877`) as the administrator with the session's key; the logging names the cause on the one redeploy (K4428).
**Fix shape.** Logging (rides the deploy): `publish_at.source` and the stated reason in the pre-flight answer, plus one structured `console.log` line `{event: "publish_at", offered, source, why}` per pre-flight (no text, no secret). Best-reading fix (unproven): `#publishAtOffer` (`ratification/index.mjs:676-686`) falls back to the profiles only when `retrieval.zone()` THROWS; `zone()` returns null without throwing, so a null from it is final. Ratification falls back to the combined profiles on a null as well as on a throw (cheap, and the asymmetry is real); and if the P read shows (i), the pre-flight answers its reason instead of null. Retrieval's change is the logging alone (`zone({why: true})`); no retrieval fix is planned. The screen change (screens-publish: three cases, three sentences, the stated reason drawn) is certain and deployable now. **Old records:** none (a read). Words: stand-ins; UX rows owed (section 5).

The only ingest-specific candidate is the cold path: `#heldTable` and `#heldTableSync` read the chunked table bytes from `calc_table_bytes` and hash them when the in-memory cache is cold (`calculations/index.mjs:593-619`), which the L probe never exercised (its table was declared in the same instance).

Because compute is not timeable inside the object, the red/green gate on the fix is the count of table bytes read and hashed on the started path (zero for an `all` ingest over 10,000 rows), a calculations unit test.
(4) Platform check **L** (the cold-restart run in Miniflare, then at the real limits) and **P** (Server-Timing of `moneyingest` against a trivial member act on a quiet copy, R-QUIET, to separate the ingest from the act floor).
**Logging that rides the deploy (K4428).** Per-stage `Server-Timing` entries inside the act, so the one redeploy names the cause: table cache hit or cold, bytes read and hashed, the picked-list build, the record insert (counts and I/O time; compute is not timeable inside the object, R-CLOCK, so the byte and call counts are the evidence). The cold-cache reading is the weaker one for the walk (declare, adopt and ingest within minutes in one busy object, the cache likely warm); the act floor is likelier, and the walker's exact case is a warm-cache ingest, which the seam run also drives. **Fix shape, by what the measure shows.** If the cold path dominates: the started path (rows "all" over `INGEST_INLINE_ROWS`) takes the row count from the table's record and reads and hashes the table bytes on the alarm leg, not in the act. If the floor dominates: no calculations change; the figure is reported to BOB for the G12 item already placed. **Old records:** none; the walker's table d4023049... and ingest a33598cb... stay (test data).

### G64 · A tab-delimited source is declared as a table of one cell per line: calculations scans comma only while the document's own reader finds the delimiter (B G45/G55 row 0; diagnosis-R7-blockers.md B4 "Row 0")

**Rows.** The row quoted at G63 (B.md:21): "Row 0 of the list is the header line ("payer\tpayee\tamount") judged as data, and 18,101 rows for 18,098 data rows: the table holds 3 extra rows (MINOR, probably my declare call giving `header` for a source that starts with its header line)". Owner **calculations**; *prov* csv-reader (existing: `delimiterSignature`, `walkRecords`/`createRecordWalk`, which find comma, semicolon, tab and pipe); seam control-plane.
**Judged a defect (BOB's call, from the diagnosis and csv-reader's code).** The walker's row 0 reads `payer\tpayee\tamount` as one string, which fits a tab-delimited file; that is an inference from how he printed it, and the first step proves it: BOB reads `op=table` for d4023049... (P) and asks the walker for the file's first bytes and sha256, which go into the START. A member can reasonably upload a tab-delimited file: csv-reader's registry reads it (the delimiter signature's candidates are comma, semicolon, tab and pipe, `csv-reader/index.mjs:289-293`), the Document screen shows it read whole, and the member then declares a table over it. Reading it as one cell per line, with every payer the whole line, and accepting it silently, is a defect of the table reader.
(1) **Cause, certain only for "the scanner reads comma only".** `calculations/tables.mjs:39-70` `csvScan` is RFC 4180 comma-only (:42-44 `44` is its only separator), and `declareTable` (`calculations/index.mjs:511-575`, the scan at :546) feeds `tableBuilder` from it; the builder drops the source's first line only when it equals the declared header cell for cell (`tables.mjs:268-272`, `same` at :270), which a one-cell line never does, so the header stays as data row 0 and every payer is a whole line (hence all rows `PAYER_NOT_IDENTIFIED`). The walker's 18,101 versus 18,098 stays OPEN: a tab file gives header + 18,098 = 18,099, not 18,101, and the builder (`tables.mjs:268-272`) also keeps a header line as row 0 for a comma file whose first line differs cell for cell (case, a rename); the `op=table` read and the file's first bytes decide which, and the failing-first test is then the walker's actual shape. A stated gap, not a deferral: the delimiter fix is complete without it.
(2) **Missed:** `test/m/calculations` declares comma files whose header matches; no test declares a source in another delimiter, and a one-cell-per-line source against a 3-column header is accepted without a word.
(3) **Failing-first.** `bio-plane/test/m/calculations/r7-delimiter.test.mjs`, through the real `tabledeclare` entry: (a) a comma file with a matching header: the table holds exactly the data rows (control, green); (b) the walker's ACTUAL shape once the first step has read it (`payer\tpayee\tamount` lines, 18,098 rows, as a constructed stand-in until then): red today (header is row 0, one cell per line); after the fix the table has the 3 declared columns, the header dropped, 18,098 rows; (c) semicolon and pipe likewise; (d) a source that does not split into the declared columns under any detected delimiter (one cell per line against a 3-column header): `tabledeclare` REFUSES with a stated reason naming the delimiter found and the columns expected, never accepts it. The seam file repeats (b) through the door as the member.
(4) Platform check **N** (parsing).
**Fix shape.** `declareTable` finds the source's delimiter with csv-reader's `delimiterSignature` over its head window and scans with `createRecordWalk(delimiter, ...)`; the canonical bytes stay comma RFC 4180 (so the same table gives the same sha); an undetermined delimiter on a one-column source against a wider header is the refusal in (d). BOB's `uses` edge (recorded here, `order.mjs` unchanged): calculations gains `csv-reader` (layer 1, below layer 5; no cycle). **Old records:** existing tables are untouched (the START says so); the walker's table d4023049... and ingest a33598cb... stay as they are (test data); any real table declared over a tab file is a one-cell-per-line table that the member re-declares; P reads the count (`op=table` of held tables with one column against a wider declared header) and reports it.

Capture the new copy / Leave it": "Untitled capture" is a MINOR wording fault (the first copy's title is "A page from httpbin.org, captured October 23, 2026") | -
(The same item in A, quoted at G65.) Owner **reevaluation, alone** (the whole fix lives in reevaluation; no hook from promotion or extraction, which would be an illegal edge up to layer 7); *prov* none; seam control-plane (cite and capture-again by the screens' own ops).
(1) **Cause unknown**; `diagnosis-R7-blockers.md` records the reproduction at the fixture shape: `bio-plane/test/m/plane/round6-reevaluation.test.mjs` (G36, real Store, spent alarm, newer capture read before and after the sweep, control with no citation) passes 3 of 3. The raising path is scheduler consumer `notice-sweep` (`scheduler/index.mjs:375-378`), then `reevaluation.noticeSweep` and `raiseNotices` (`reevaluation/index.mjs:2284-2370`, `:2640-2670`). What differs from the walk: (i) the walker cited "paragraph 0" through the screen (`citation`/cite) while the test marks a passage with `contentmint` and writes the basis itself, so the leg's `content_id` may not exist on the live path (the sweep reads `inquiry_basis` legs with a `content_id`, :2288-2293); (ii) a notice is raised only when the newer copy grades `affected` or `undetermined` (an unchanged paragraph 0 raises none, correctly); (iii) the newer capture's reading may still be owed when the sweep runs, and `chain_unread` raises nothing (:2279-2282, `unread` counted at :2316-2317); the sweep delay is 1 s (`REEVAL_NOTICE_DELAY_MS`, :114). **Best reading of (iii), from code now read:** `#sweepPending` (`reevaluation/index.mjs:2616-2621`) holds the sweep pending after a completed pass only "when a receipt was counted since the last complete pass began" (`receipt_seq > complete_seq`); a receipt is written when the capture arrives, which is BEFORE the member's press files it as a document and before its reading is made. A sweep that runs between the receipt and the reading finds the leg's chain unread (or no newer bundle), completes its pass, and nothing counted afterwards re-opens it: the reading finishing, or the document being filed, is not a receipt. The walk (acquire 2.0 s, then promote, press to finished 5.5 s) fits that order. (iv) the live alarm trouble of the earlier rounds.
(2) **Missed:** the G36 seam test builds the citation by its own acts instead of the screen's, and reads the newer capture BEFORE the sweep or lets the sweep run after it; no test orders receipt, sweep, then filing and reading.
(3) **Failing-first.** `bio-plane/test/m/reevaluation/r7-sweep-unread.test.mjs` (unit, real store): a leg on a passage; a receipt counted; the sweep runs with the newer capture unread (`chain_unread` 1, raised 0); the newer capture is then filed and read; the next tick must run the sweep again and raise the notice (red now: the sweep is not pending). In the seam file, the walker's case through the door: acquire `https://www.example.gov/page?x=1` (scripted outbound, the body changes in paragraph 0 between fetches), file it, cite its paragraph 0 in a question by the SAME op the Cite press sends, capture again through the "Capture the new copy" press' op, let the alarm run; assert `op=queue` holds `FINDING::newer-capture-affects-reference::...` for the question. If the seam test is green and the unit test red, the best reading is confirmed; if both are green the logging below decides.
(4) Platform check **P** after the redeploy: read `owedwork` for the `notice-sweep` consumer's last tick and the question's basis legs on biosmoke8 (a worker with the administrator session); **L** is the seam test in Miniflare.
**Logging that rides the deploy (K4428).** Each `noticeSweep` tick's `examined`, `unread` and `raised` (they exist in `raiseNotices`' answer, :2325-2330) are written by reevaluation as one structured line `{event: "notice_sweep", examined, unread, raised}` (the scheduler's tick answer already carries the same object, so no scheduler change); the walker's INQ-2026-0029 leg's `content_id` is read at the P step.
**Fix shape (best reading, unproven until the seam test is red).** Three passes at a 1 s delay would be 3 s and miss any file whose reading takes minutes, so the bound is by STATE, not by pass count: `#sweepPending` stays true while any leg of the last pass was `chain_unread` AND the newer capture's reading is still owed (reevaluation polls the unread legs at its delay; it learns that the reading is still owed from its OWN legal reads, `text-chain` and `content`: the newer bundle exists and has no reading row, with an AGE CAP so a leg stuck unread is not polled for ever; no new `uses` edge, and the plane ledger and extraction are not read), and goes false when no leg is unread or the owed reading has ended (read or final); no loop for a leg whose chain stays unread with nothing owed. **Old records:** none; a leg already past a completed pass is reconsidered by the first sweep after the deploy (the job states how).

### G74 · A member files a CSV into a project she is not in: the screen shows the project's id, then her own Document page says "Nothing is here" (A G15/G41; diagnosis-R7-reading.md R6). BOB's decision (P17): a member may file into a project only when she is its member or invited to it

**Rows.**
{{A25}}
Owner **promotion** (the refusal at its op `promote`); **screens-intake (S)** (the filing picker and the shown name); *prov* membership (existing: `notAParticipant` R87, `participation`); seam control-plane.
**BOB's decision, P17, following from Bob's K4557** ("they must be a member of the project to even draft a records request if it's to be associated with the project"; gathering stays open to an invited, not yet joined member): a member may file a document into a project only when she is its member or invited to it. The filing picker offers only those projects. `promote` with any other project is refused at the door with a member's refusal in DEC-83's pattern (the sentence, then the remedy: only the project's members can add to it; ask to join). The refusal key's row is owed to UX-DESIGN (section 5); the code carries a stand-in sentence. BOB records the ruling in `rulings.md` at adoption.
(1) **Cause, certain.** The id: `screens-intake/capture.mjs:58` `title: (pm && field(pm,"title")) || project`; the project's front-matter read (`image`) is refused for a non-member, so the id is the fallback. The filing is accepted: the upload files with front matter `project: <id>` (`capture.mjs:124`), `promote` takes the project from the body or query (`control-plane/index.mjs:3276-3277`) and promotion's **R53** commits "every bundle ... with the project its document states", fenced by that project's sight (`membership.viewerPredicate`, its R43); nothing in promotion refuses a filer who is neither a member nor invited (promotion's requirements state no such refusal, `build/requirements/promotion.md` R53, R19), so the document is committed under a project she cannot see and her own Document read answers "Nothing is here". The Add screen's picker offers a project she cannot read.
(2) **Missed:** the intake tests filed as the project's owner or the administrator; no test acted as a member outside the project, or opened the Add screen on a project route she cannot read.
(3) **Failing-first.** The walker's case, through the door in the seam file: a member not in the administrator's project files a CSV naming that project (from the Add screen's route, `captureupload` then `promote`); today it is ACCEPTED (red), after the fix `promote` is refused with the member's refusal and no bundle is written. Controls (green today and after): a joined member files; an invited, not yet joined member files (gathering stays open, K4557). `bio-plane/test/m/promotion/r7-foreign-project.test.mjs`: the same at the op, the refusal carrying a key whose sentence and remedy are in the stand-in row. `bio-plane/test/m/screens-intake/r7-project-picker.test.mjs`: the picker offers only projects the member belongs to or is invited to, and the filed line never shows an id.
(4) Platform check **L** (the Miniflare door run) and **P** at the re-walk: the member's Add screen on the walker's project is refused, a joined and an invited member file.
**Fix shape.** promotion refuses at `promote` using membership's participation (member or invited allowed); screens-intake's picker lists only those projects and the shown name is the name where the read gives it, never the id. **Old records:** INFO-2026-0161 (c1.csv) stays as it is (the walker's test data; no migration, no refile by code).

K4269 basis, stated: this is a dropped title in the owning module (code), and two rows are indistinguishable in the Held list when both read "Untitled capture" (a member cannot tell which document to open), so it is not a misused word; it is a data defect, not wording only.

**Fix shape.** capture stores the delivered name in `bundles.title` on the fetch-delivery path (or `heldAt` answers the R54 chain); `held.mjs` falls back to the chain, not the word. The word "A page from..." for a PDF is wording only (section 5). **Old records:** held records without a title read through the chain at draw time; no rewrite.

Filed here as well. It stays held under Walk T51 rewalk5 A." (names the other walker's project, not mine; MINOR wording) at 2.1 s | shots/A/addr-dena-p2-1280.png
Owner **capture** (`:754`, `:763`); *prov* **acquisition** (`keepWhole`, `keepPast`, `:510`/`:1458`); seam capture's `r7-upload-t51.test.mjs`.
**Product share versus proxy** (the walker's wall minus `do-` is the proxy plus transfer): 24 MiB 20.7 - 18.4 = 2.3 s; 10.3 MB PDF 22.8 - 20.3 = 2.5 s; 40 MB 25.7 - 23.2 = 2.5 s; dena 22.4 - 17.6 = 4.8 s; `in-captureupload` includes the body's arrival (streamed, `control-plane/index.mjs:3073-3078`), about 3 s at 24 MiB and 5 s at 40 MB at the measured 8 MB/s. The product's share is 12-17 s on each, so the bars are missed by the product, not the link. A's acadia wall (14.4 s below its `do-acquire` 15.2 s) is a clock taken after the request started: not compared.
(1) **Cause, certain from code, the time by arithmetic (not measured inside).** Uploads: `uploadCapture` writes each 8 MiB part one at a time (digest, `head`, `put`, `capture/index.mjs:700-720`), then for 2-4 parts calls `keepWhole(ev, {sha, parts, total})` with NO `bytes` (`capture/index.mjs:754`), so acquisition reads every part back, re-hashes it, hashes the whole, and puts the whole (`acquisition/index.mjs:460-485`): roughly twice the store traffic, before the answer. Above `MULTIPART_KEEP_MAX` (32 MiB) it AWAITS `keepPast` within `boundMs - KEEP_RESERVE_MS - elapsed` (`capture/index.mjs:763`; `acquisition/index.mjs:437-445`, bound 25 s, reserve 5 s): the 40 MB upload's `in-` 22.5 s is that wait plus about 2.5 s of parts, so it ran to its bound by design (R103/F2). The first 12 MB upload (no tick then) took 2.3-3.3 s; the later ones ran beside reading ticks in the same object, which delays every store answer (G82); contention is part of it, not isolated. Web captures: the render is waited a fixed `RENDER_WAIT_MS` = 10 s after the fetch (`acquisition/index.mjs:510`, :1458; fetch up to `FETCH_WAIT_MS` 10 s, :399), not what is left of the 15 s bar: dena `in-acquire` 16.8 s, Wikipedia 15.2 s, acadia 12.1 s (its render also failed, G72). A quick page (B: httpbin, `in-acquire` 1.8 s) is fine.
(2) **Missed:** upload tests run in memory (zero latency) and count bytes, not store calls or elapsed time; no test fixes the bars (2 s / 15 s) against a store with R2-like latency; the render wait is a constant tested alone.
(3) **Failing-first.** `bio-plane/test/m/capture/r7-upload-t51.test.mjs`: a 3-part 24 MiB stream through `captureupload` (control-plane door) over a store charging 400-800 ms per get/put of 8 MiB on a fake clock (the test's own, R-CLOCK): the answer makes no `get` of a part and at most (parts x put + one whole put); a 5-part 40 MB stream is answered without waiting for `putFromParts` (handed to `keepLater` at once, `keep_pending`). `bio-plane/test/m/acquisition/r7-t51.test.mjs`: a fetch of 6 s and a render that never ends: `acquire` answers within 15 s minus the promote reserve.

The job EXTENDS the existing `putFromParts` (`record-core/index.mjs:1905`) and acquisition's `keepFromParts` (`acquisition/index.mjs:409`, used above 32 MiB today) to the upload path and to 2-4 parts (capture passes no `bytes` today); it does not build them. Handing the keep to the ledger at once above 32 MiB reverses R103/F2's "await it"; the ledger keeps it alive so the reason holds, and the Document for such a file is "stated" anyway.
**Fix shape.** Pass the parts' buffers or use `putFromParts` for any multipart and hand the keep to the ledger at once (answer after the parts are stored); the render wait is 15 s minus elapsed minus the reserve (the "still being made, filed as a second document" path already exists). **Old records:** none.

## 0. Rules for every START (round 7 section 0, and round 6's, hold; in short)

- **R-FOUR** (K4426): each cause has (1) root cause with file:line, or "Cause unknown" with the diagnosis file that records the reproduction tried (the entry then plans the logging that names it on the one redeploy, K4428, and the best-reading fix), (2) why the tests missed it and that gap fixed, (3) a failing-first test on the walker's exact case through the real entry path, seen red first, (4) the platform check: **L** local workerd or a capped child at the real limits, **P** read-only probe of biosmoke8 (through the proxy only), **N** none.
- **R-SEAM / R-ONE** (K4427): one owning job per cause, with its providers; a through-the-door test uses the real merged providers and inputs made by the member's own acts, never a hand-written answer shape (the E4 lesson: generate a fixture from the real op); the walker's row is copied verbatim into the START (this plan quotes it in the cause). A seam test whose provider is unmerged is written first and stays red by name. This round's door tests live in ONE file, `bio-plane/test/m/control-plane/t51-round8-seam.test.mjs` (Miniflare plane, the pattern of `t51-round7-seam.test.mjs`), written by the control-plane job; each section names its providers. The counting-door tests (`r8-words-door`, `r8-bootstrap-fanout`, `r8-hops`, `r8-caseratify-calls`, and the seam file's count section for G115/G116, review2 D3) use one helper, `wrap-entry.mjs` (the plane's default export wrapped so every Durable Object call by route and every R2 call of a request is counted and timed), written once by the control-plane job and read by the others.
- **R-NOTMET / R-OLD / R-LIVE** (K4427, K4428): no "not met" holds a cause; stored-data fixes say "new data only" or carry a repair; platform causes start with a measurement; one deployment after every job is merged and BOB's check of K4426's four points, carrying the logging for every cause still unknown; then a targeted re-walk.
- **R-STOP.** A "reading follows" test asserts the words are in `text_units` (and the last line searchable), never only that a reading exists.
- **R-CLOCK.** Workerd freezes `Date.now()` during synchronous work, so no timing taken inside the object sees compute. A test asserts bytes pulled, calls made, rows read and written and ledger rows, never a compute time from the object's clock; a wall time is taken by the test itself outside the object and only where this plan says so. Where a wait is an await (a scanner call, an R2 trip), a time is valid because the wait is real.
- **R-PROXY** (K4733): the copy is reached through the environment's proxy only, unchanged (Chromium `proxy: {server: HTTPS_PROXY}`, curl and node as configured). The direct route (`--noproxy`, unset proxy variables, a private trust store) is refused by auto mode and never tried. The proxy's own share is read beside `Server-Timing` and is not the product's: 1.0-4.0 s to first byte on a new connection (about half of them), 0.9-1.4 s a call in the browser, about 8 MB/s on an upload and about 0.5 MB/s measured down for a 6.3 MB body.
- **R-QUIET.** The PERFORMANCE re-time runs after this round's redeploy on a quiet copy (no walker's bulk upload and no reading tick of a large file in the same object); a time taken while a tick runs is reported as that, not as a screen's time.
- **R-NOLOAD** (K4787, new): a test red on a busy machine is a fragile test fixed to assert work or a same-run control, or a product defect when slow; every heavy file named below runs alone, five runs, and the time-sensitive set also on one core (`taskset -c 0`); a count asserted through the door against the Queue's 5 s bucket allows the buckets the case's own wall crossed, and the seam file's count section and the no-tick control of G106 are among the five alone-runs (review2 G2, G5).
- **R-LOG** (K4428, new in its strictness): the logging a cause plans is itself tested (the line exists, carries the named fields, and is written BEFORE the slow await as well as after, because Workers Logs lost the tail of every long firing's line, `diagnosis-R8-watchdog.md` Finding 4); a line is a count, an instant or an identifier, never a secret.
- **R-SILENT** (K4571, K4630): a new bound is stated to the member in the same change; a bound that changes what a member may do is Bob's (K1881) and the fix does not raise or lower one on its own. The standard's figures (K4628) are the bounds; reaching them is engineering.

**Your entries, full text (the walker's rows are quoted verbatim; each cause needs your (1)-(4)):**

### G114 · The words answer is fetched once per sign-in, not once per browser per release: `vary: Authorization` keys the cache by the bearer token, and the door reads whole bodies it does not need (B NEW MAJOR; diagnosis-R8-speed.md A; BOB #269's direction, Bob's question why the word table is not kept for a year)

**Rows.** The row quoted at G113 (B.md:23). Owner **control-plane** (`words-cache.mjs`; R116 amended: "the same release's words are fetched once per browser per release, across sign-ins"); *prov* translations (G113; a `words_version` stamp of the team's adoptions, review2 D1 (c)), interface-shell (job 11: `loadWords` sends `v`), instance-setup (job 9: `bootstrap` carries the viewer's language and the stamp). "Fixed" means exactly that: a second sign-in in the same browser, same release, does not fetch the words again.
(1) **Cause, certain.** The shell names the release (`interface-shell/browser.mjs:2641-2648`, meta `cs-version`), so the answer carries `private, max-age=31536000, immutable` and is gzipped (`control-plane/words-cache.mjs:25-41`; reproduced: with `v` 1,066,983 B and the headers, without `v` 6,353,380 B and no cache header), but `words-cache.mjs:32` (`headers.set("vary", "Authorization, Accept-Encoding")`, with `cache-control: private, max-age=WORDS_MAX_AGE, immutable`; `diagnosis-R8-speed.md` cites it as :38) makes the cache key include the request's `Authorization` and every sign-in has a new bearer token, so the browser's cache never matches across sign-ins: one fetch per sign-in, against R116's "not sent again to a caller that holds them". A call without `v` (the walker's three calls from the page) is neither cached nor compressed. The Worker's whole-body reads of an answer that is not a refusal (`control-plane/index.mjs:142-147, 160-163`) cost bytes for no purpose on this op. The words answer holds the team's adopted words (`translations/index.mjs:722-737`: `shown` from `#languageHistories`), which is why the adoptions version belongs in the key (review2 D1).
(2) **Missed:** the words-cache tests (`fixround2.test.mjs`) did not exercise `Vary: Authorization` with two sign-ins; nothing counts the Worker's whole-body reads.
(3) **Failing-first.** (i) The provider's own (review2 D1): `interface-shell/r8-words-version.test.mjs`: the request `loadWords` issues names the release, the language and the adoptions version in `v`; a language change or a changed adoptions version changes `v` (red: `v` is the release alone, `browser.mjs:2641-2648`). (ii) A real-Chromium L test beside `r8-words-door.test.mjs` (the served shell as built, Miniflare plane, the G77 pattern): two sign-ins in ONE browser context, same release, language and adoptions version, then the second sign-in's `interfacewords` is answered from the browser's cache or 304 on the first's validator with no body (red: a full 200 with a new token); two members with different languages in ONE browser context each get their own words, an adoption mid-release is seen at the next load, and the stale stamp is never served after `translationadopt` (review D7, review2 D1); at the door, the answer carries the same `etag` and no `vary: authorization` for the two tokens, the object is asked no more than once for the pair, and a `v` that does not carry the current release, language and adoptions version is answered without the year; the tallies skip the body of an answer that is not a refusal (count of whole-body reads, red: 2 per call). How a non-refusal is known without reading the body (review2 N5): by the object's status and headers (a 200 whose `content-type` and the op's own header mark the word table); a refusal from this op is a non-200 or lacks the header and is still read; a test sends a 200 `{ok: false}` body without the header and it is still tallied (today `tallySessionRefusal`, `control-plane/index.mjs:142-147`, reads the JSON to find `ok: false`).
(4) Platform check **P** (a second sign-in answered from the cache on biosmoke8) and **N** for the headers.
**Fix shape (review D7, K4811 (7), K4814 (1), review2 D1).** The cache key is the release, the viewer's language and the team's word adoptions: a language change, an adoption (`translationadopt`/`translationconfirm`) or another member on the same browser fetches afresh, so no stale words are served for a year. The shell sends all three in `v` (job 11, interface-shell: `loadWords` names the release, the language and the adoptions version, where today it sends the release alone, `interface-shell/browser.mjs:2641-2648`, the meta `cs-version` being the release, `interface-shell/index.mjs:495`). The answer keeps `private, max-age=31536000, immutable`; `vary` drops `Authorization` because the URL (`v`) now carries the language and the adoptions version; the door's validator (`words-cache.mjs:23`, today `v !== env.VERSION`) accepts exactly a `v` that carries the current release and the asked language and adoptions version, and an old or wrong `v` is answered without the year. **Where the shell gets the language and the stamp (review2 D1 (c); BOB's detail, recorded at adoption; recommended):** translations states a `words_version` stamp of the team's current adoptions and confirmations (it changes at `translationadopt` and `translationconfirm`), and `bootstrap` (instance-setup, job 9) carries it with the viewer's language to the shell, which reads it before it names `v`; the interface-shell job finds the order of `loadWords` against that read and, if `loadWords` runs first, the first load sends the release alone and is answered `private, no-cache` (one round trip), later loads sending all three. The whole-body reads of the tallies are skipped for this op (N5 above). The session gate stays (K4807); the question whether the words may be served without a session is dropped. **Old records:** none.

### G101 · A table is declared from an index of the file (the held units' joined text) rather than from the file's bytes (B G64 STILL; diagnosis-R8-reading.md C "Fix shape")

**Rows.**
> **B.md:13** · G64 | a tab-delimited copy of the table declared; `op=table`; the file's first bytes | 1280 | member | new | the same data (payer PBs000000.., payee P002, amount 1..97) as a comma file (INFO-2026-0456, rw7-s.csv) and as a tab file (INFO-2026-0457, rw7-s.tsv), each uploaded through Add > file, `contentmint`, `tabledeclare` with the 3 declared fields, `op=table` | FIXED for the tab file; STILL for the comma file's row count (MAJOR) | tab file, first bytes `payer\tpayee\tamount\nPBs000000\tP002\t1\n`: declared with 3 columns (payer, payee, amount), `rows` 18,098, `undetermined.count` 0 (table c0f8687a...). Comma file, first bytes `payer,payee,amount\nPBs000000,P002,1\n`: `rows` 18,100 (two more than the 18,098 data rows), `undetermined.count` 2: cells at row 7317 and row 14636, column amount, hold the text "amount" (the header line again, read as data), so the two tables do NOT share a sha (3217...  vs c0f8...) although the data is the same; same two extra rows in an earlier pair (rw7-c.csv 18,100; rw7-t.tsv 18,098). The old walker's "18,101 for 18,098" (header row judged as data, three extra) stays open: now 2 extras that sit at the reading's piece boundaries (7317 - 0 = 7317, 14636 - 7317 = 7319 rows apart), which fits a CSV read in windows whose reading text repeats the header line at each window (G68's window); I did not read the reading text itself to confirm. `op=table d4023049...` no longer exists on the copy (`found: false`; the old record). Declare 1.2-2.8 s, upload+file 8-11 s | -
Owner **calculations**; *prov* content (G100), csv-reader (existing: `delimiterSignature`, `createRecordWalk`).
(1) **Cause, certain.** `declareTable` reads the source rows through `content.passageText` (`calculations/index.mjs:507`, `#sourceRows` :486) and falls back to the stored CSV bytes only when `passageText` is not a string (:508-514); for a `document` source whose container is `csv` it therefore makes the table from the reading's units, which are an index shaped for search and citation (blocks, caps, repeated headers), not the file. G100 removes the duplicated header from the index; this cause is that a table must be the file's whatever the index holds (the table's sha is the user's evidence of what the file said).
(2) **Missed:** the same stub as G100: `calculations/fixture.mjs`'s `w.csv(text)` hands the text whole, so no calculations test ever saw an index that differs from the file.
(3) **Failing-first.** `calculations/r8-source.test.mjs` with the real content in place of the stub: a `document` source whose container is `csv` is declared from the stored bytes and its table's sha equals the sha of the same rows declared from the tab route; a source whose bytes are not held (no whole object) is refused stated rather than made from the index (red today: made from the index). The seam test of G100 is the through-the-door proof and covers both.
(4) Platform check **N**.
**Fix shape.** `declareTable` reads a CSV `document` source's stored bytes with csv-reader's walk (the delimiter fix of round 7 stays), and uses the index only for a source with no bytes of its own; the canonical bytes stay comma RFC 4180. **Old records (review2 N6):** tables declared before the fix from a CSV over 128 KiB hold the extra header rows; P counts them (tables whose `undetermined.count` equals the number of blocks minus one and whose cells read the header text), and a money ingest over such a table is not run.

**Rows.**

Nothing was kept; try again." Yet `heldat` shows the file kept (INFO-2026-0480, retrieved 20:22:49, the start) and a `fetch-done` arrived. Nothing was kept; try again" (same flip as G85). The plain "filed under" line was not reached | shots/A/g73-member-0115-1280.png
Owner **acquisition** (R46: `openCaptures` compares the member's id, not a stamp form); S control-plane; *prov* none new. Behaviour, not wording.
(1) **Cause, certain; reproduced (`probeA`).** The screen's watch ends the capture as failed after three polls in which the session is not listed and the record shows no copy (`screens-intake/capture.mjs:730-736`: `if (missed + 1 < 3) return again(missed + 1)`, then `refusal.FETCH_FAILED`; the poll is `cs.later(4000)` for the first ten, :711). For a member the session is never listed: the plane parks the fetch with `opened_by: askingMember` (`acquisition/index.mjs:944, 1290`), `sessMember` being the folded member id (`probe-dee`; the founder's is `admin`), handed through the capture door (`capture/ops.mjs:161`, `plane/door.mjs:93-94`, `capture/index.mjs:3348`). `opencaptures`' `by` is not that: the door stamps every declared `by` with the viewer's form (`control-plane/index.mjs:1356-1357`, `actor = viaSession ? sessViewer`; a session's viewer is `member:<id>`, `credentials/index.mjs:520`, and `admin` for the founder), and `openCaptures` keeps a session only when `st.opened_by === by` (`acquisition/index.mjs:2390`). `member:probe-dee` is not `probe-dee`, so `captures: []` for every ordinary member; for the founder both are `admin`, which is why walker A's administrator screen stayed "being fetched". The wall time follows from the code: each poll is 4 s of timer plus two calls (`opencaptures`, `heldat`) of 0.6-1.4 s through the proxy, three polls = 16.5-20.4 s (the walker saw 18.2 s). When the screen concludes it also stops watching, so the member is never told when the file arrives, which K4562 requires ("informed when the download completes"). The Queue's `fetch-done` does arrive because the delivery reads `opened_by` as stored (`control-plane/index.mjs:783-786`; `recordFetchDone` is called at :826-832, review2 N1). Add's "Unfinished" line (R31) has therefore listed nothing for any ordinary member since T50, subresource sessions included (`acquisition/index.mjs:1573, 2111`): the same cause and the same fix.
(2) **Missed:** every `openCaptures` test calls the function with a hand-written `by` equal to the stored id (`fix6-t51.test.mjs:210`, `fix5-render-owed.test.mjs:51`: `by: "m1"`), and the screen test stubs `opencaptures` with a hand-written listing (`screens-intake/t51-fix6.test.mjs:36`): nothing put the door's stamp and the stored id side by side (the E4 lesson). Round 7's seam tests of a member's large fetch (G61's twin, G75) wait for the Queue and `heldcaptures`, never for `opencaptures`. Gap fixed by the listing test below and the rule that a screen-watch test takes its listing from the real door.
(3) **Failing-first.** Seam file section A, "R8 (A1): an enrolled member's large capture is listed by `opencaptures` from the press until the document is delivered": `person("r8-dee", "member", ["contribute"])` presses `acquire` on a 14 MB PDF served with `Content-Length` at 2 MB/s (the `serve(..., {rate})` helper); 200 and `fetch_pending.session`; `op=opencaptures` polled as her every second until `heldat` says `held: true` (at most 120 s): at EVERY poll before that the list contains a capture with that session and `fetch_pending` (red today: `captures: []` at every poll), and after delivery it does not (control: the founder's identical press passes today). Second test, "R8 (A1b): the Add screen's watch over the real plane ends in the finished line": `CAPTURE_SCREEN.parts.watchFetch` with a `cs` whose `call` is the door's (`screenCall(dee)`) and whose `later(ms, fn)` calls `fn` after 1 s, over `{captured: {fetching: {session}}}`: `promoteRefused` is never set, `captured.bundleId` is the filed bundle after delivery, and `opencaptures` was asked more than three times before it (red: `refusal.FETCH_FAILED` at the third poll with the fetch still running). Unit: `acquisition/r8-open-captures.test.mjs`: a session parked with `opened_by: "m1"` is listed for `by: "member:m1"` and for `by: "m1"`, not for `member:m2`; a session of the founder (`admin`) is listed for `admin`.
(4) Platform check **L** done (Miniflare, the real Worker, object and alarm; the listing is a pure comparison) and **P** at the re-walk: an enrolled member's 14-16 MB govinfo press, the Add screen watched to the finished line (it says "being fetched", keeps saying it, then says "Fetched" with a link when the document is filed, at whatever minute that is).
**Fix shape.** One comparison in acquisition: the member's id is read out of the viewer's form by the one shared predicate (the spelling the G58 fix uses for the root administrator: `baseViewer`/`viewerMember`, `member-roster/index.mjs:45, 59`; BOB records the `uses` edge acquisition -> member-roster in `modules.json` with `MODULE_ORDER`, K657, legal because lower; review G10), so both the stored bare id and the stamped form match; the door's stamp stays the viewer's form as the whole op surface has it. **Old records:** none (a read).

### G104 · The state between "made" and "delivered" is listed for no one: `owedFetch` saves the session with `queue: []` and `openCaptures` skips a session with fewer than one outstanding file (A G85; diagnosis-R8-capture.md A2 (a))

**Rows.** The rows quoted at G103 (A.md:15). Owner **acquisition** (R105); S control-plane.
(1) **Cause, certain; reproduced (`probeB`, `probeA`).** Once the alarm has made the capture, `owedFetch` saves the session whole with `queue: []` and `fetch_done` (`acquisition/index.mjs:2354-2361`), and `openCaptures` skips a session with fewer than one outstanding file (`if (outstanding < 1 ...) continue` at :2393; :2391 is the `const outstanding`, review2 N1). From that moment until the delivery drops the session (:2365) the session is listed for no one, the founder included: the delivery (promotion, reading follow, the `fetch-done` row) takes a few seconds when it works, and when it is slow the screen's three misses (G103) fire inside it.
(2) **Missed:** `owedFetch` tests deliver with a stub `deliver` that accepts (`r7-fetchdone.test.mjs`, `fix6-t51.test.mjs`) and look at the result, never at the listing in between.
(3) **Failing-first.** `acquisition/r8-open-captures.test.mjs` also asserts a session in the made state (queue empty, `fetch_done` present) is listed with `fetch_pending` until it is dropped (red: listed for no one); the seam test of G103 polls through the made state with a delivery held for 5 s by the test.
(4) Platform check **L** (Miniflare, the real alarm) and **P** (as G103).
**Fix shape.** `openCaptures` lists a made-but-undelivered session as `fetch_pending` with a `delivering` mark; the screen's line for it is the existing "being fetched". **Old records:** none.

### G105 · A delivery the record refuses is retried without end and never said: `owedFetch` turns every refusal into `DELIVERY_REFUSED` (A G73 member NEW MAJOR; diagnosis-R8-capture.md A2 (b))

**Rows.**
> **A.md:26** · G73 | same as the member who cannot read its home project | 1280 | member | old | same press | NEW (MAJOR) | the member cannot see the held copy, so the press starts a new `acquire` of the 41.5 MB file (`fetch_pending`) and after 20 s the screen says "The page couldn't be fetched: the site didn't answer in time. Nothing was kept; try again" (same flip as G85). The plain "filed under" line was not reached | shots/A/g73-member-0115-1280.png
> **A.md:46** · Counts: reading owed 5 final 0; subresources owed 0 final 1; fetch owed 1 final 0 (old rows of others: the 41.5 MB member press may be one). `last_run` 1.7 s old, `alarm_at` +0.1 s, `oldest_overdue` now, `watchdog_seen` 77 s old, `stale` false. By `&sha=` none of my documents has a row left.
Owner **acquisition** (R105: `owedFetch` ends a delivery refused for a code no later try changes, stating it in `fetch_pending.failed`); *prov* control-plane (`deliverOwedRender` already returns `{ok: false, reason}`, the refusal code is returned at `control-plane/index.mjs:808`, `:830-831` is the end of the function, review D10; no change expected), screens-intake (R58: the watch already draws `failed` by its words, and `refusal.CAPTURE_HELD_BY_ANOTHER_BUNDLE` has its catalogue row C-53.13); S control-plane.
(1) **Cause, certain; reproduced (`probeB`, `probeB2`, `probeC`).** A member who presses an address whose bytes the record already holds under a document she cannot see (the 41.5 MB budget book, held in another walker's project) gets `fetch_pending` (the pre-flight `heldat` cannot see the copy, `capture.mjs:584-594`; the body's digest is not known until it is read), the alarm downloads it, and `deliverOwedRender` to `promoteDocument` is refused `CAPTURE_HELD_BY_ANOTHER_BUNDLE` (C-53.13). `owedFetch` turns any refused delivery into `{ok: true, done: false, outstanding: 1, reason: "DELIVERY_REFUSED"}` (`acquisition/index.mjs:2363-2364`) and the plane's `fetch` consumer files that as an attempt with the ledger's back-off (`plane/pending.mjs:243-252`). The row never ends (still owed at 118 s of a 3 s download; attempts counting; `why DELIVERY_REFUSED`, `next_try` +30 s), nothing says why (the reason is dropped), the member's Queue is empty, and the screen says "couldn't be fetched, nothing was kept" (G103) while the bytes were kept. The end-of-walk ledger "fetch owed 1 final 0" (A.md:46) is this row.
(2) **Missed:** `owedFetch` tests deliver with an accepting stub, and the seam's fetch tests press an address nobody else holds; no test pressed an address already held elsewhere through the alarm. Gap fixed by the held-elsewhere test below and the rule that a refusal of the delivery names its code in the ledger row and in the session.
(3) **Failing-first.** Seam file section A2, "R8 (A2): a member's large capture of bytes held in a project she cannot see ends in words, not in a retry": the founder files a 12 MB PDF in a project (parked, delivered by the alarm); an enrolled member not in the project presses the same address and gets `fetch_pending`; within 60 s `owedwork&sha=<session>` shows the row ended (`state` not `owed`, or `final`) with `why` = `CAPTURE_HELD_BY_ANOTHER_BUNDLE` and not `DELIVERY_REFUSED`; `opencaptures` as the member lists the session with `fetch_pending.failed: "CAPTURE_HELD_BY_ANOTHER_BUNDLE"`; the watch (G103's double) ends with `promoteRefused` holding that code's words (`refusalHtml`), not `refusal.FETCH_FAILED`'s "Nothing was kept" (red today: owed for ever, nothing listed); control (green before and after): the same member pressing an address nobody holds is delivered and gets `fetch-done`. Unit: `acquisition/r8-fetch-refused.test.mjs`: `owedFetch` with a `deliver` answering `{ok: false, reason: "CAPTURE_HELD_BY_ANOTHER_BUNDLE"}` ends `final` and saves `fetch_pending.failed` with that code; with `{ok: false, reason: "DELIVERY_FAILED"}` it goes again (a transient failure still retries).
(4) Platform check **L** done (Miniflare, `probeB2`) and **P** at the re-walk: the 41.5 MB (0115) pressed by the enrolled member who cannot see its home: the screen ends in the held-elsewhere words within a minute of the download, the row ends, no endless retry of a 41 MB delivery.
**Fix shape (review G11).** The same swallow is at three sites, `acquisition/index.mjs:2272`, `:2290` (the render-delivery paths) and `:2364`: all three are fixed, each with a unit case in `r8-fetch-refused.test.mjs`. A refusal code in the set that no later try changes (held by another bundle, a refused kind, a size over the bound) ends the row and is saved on the session; a transient code still retries with the ledger's back-off. The gentler sentence UX may want is a `later-ux.md` row (section 5), not a job. **Old records:** the one owed `fetch` row on the copy ends at the first tick after the fix; P reads it.

### G108 · A project-less capture says "Fetched. ... is filed under ." with an empty fill (A G85 NEW MINOR; diagnosis-R8-capture.md D1)

**Rows.**
