(blank project); "Already in the record as A page from www.govinfo.gov ..." though the held title is BUDGET-2021-APP.pdf | shots/A/g75-2021-again-1280.png
Owner **screens-intake**. Behaviour: the code chooses a key that cannot be filled; the new sentence for the no-project case is wording (section 5).
(1) **Cause, certain.** The watch's finished line draws `screen.capture.fetch.done` ("Fetched. {document} is filed under {project}.", `setup-words/rows.mjs:11149`) with `project: g.filed && g.filed.id ? g.filed.title : ""` (`screens-intake/capture.mjs:334-337`); a capture made with no project has `filed: null` (:665), so the placeholder is empty. The `keyed(...)` guard at :322 only catches a missing key.
(2) **Missed:** the G2 screen tests (`t51-fix6.test.mjs:36-70`) all carry a project (`PROJ`); no test finishes a project-less fetch.
(3) **Failing-first** (review2 D3: no test imports a later module). `screens-intake/r8-fetched-line.test.mjs`, the screen half with `capture`'s own ops (screens-intake uses `capture`) over `capture`'s fixture: a project-less capture (no subject on the route) answered `fetch_pending` and then delivered draws a finished line that contains the document's link and none of the empty fill (the expected text read from the word table, not a copy of the sentence, review N4, K3810); control: with a project it draws the project's name. The half "watched to delivery over the real plane's answers" (the seam's `screenCall`) is a section of the control-plane seam file, red by name until acquisition and screens-intake have merged.
(4) Platform check **N** (a count of drawn text); **P** the member's second press at the re-walk.
**Fix shape.** A key for the no-project case (stand-in sentence in code: "Fetched. {document} waits in Held captures"), chosen when `filed` is null. **Old records:** none.

### G109 · "Already in the record as A page from ..." names the compared copy by the fallback chain, never the stored `copy.title` (A G85 NEW MINOR; diagnosis-R8-capture.md D2)

**Rows.** The row quoted at G108 (A.md:16). Owner **screens-intake** (R38, R54, R58); *prov* capture (R96, existing: `heldat` answers `copy.title`).
(1) **Cause, certain from the code (the held title's source is the P read below).** R38's row names the compared copy from the vouched picker list read when the screen opened (`D.held`), else from a fallback chain of host and day (`capture.mjs:376-380`: `heldTitle(cs, row || {source: {address, retrieved}})`). A document delivered after the screen opened (the member's own just-finished fetch) is not in that list, so the row falls back to the chain "A page from www.govinfo.gov, captured ..." and never reads `copy.title`, the title `heldat` answers for exactly this purpose (`capture/index.mjs:1482`; capture R96's last sentence; screens-intake R54 "the stored title wins"). The G75 fix added the field and the held list's fallback, not this use (A.md:29 passes for the old 0159 only because its row was in `D.held`). The finished line uses the title computed at the press from a document not yet delivered (:664, :739), so it too can differ from the stored title.
(2) **Missed:** the R38 tests (`t51-fix*.test.mjs`) put the document in the preloaded `D.held`; none gives a `heldat` copy whose row is absent from it.
(3) **Failing-first** (review2 D3). `screens-intake/r8-fetched-line.test.mjs`, second case, with `capture`'s own ops: `heldat` answers a `copy.title` for a delivered document that is absent from `D.held`, the screen was opened BEFORE the delivery, the address pressed again: the row's name equals `heldat`'s `copy.title` (it contains the stored title and not "A page from"; red today); the finished line names the document by the stored title. The alarm-delivered half (the member's project-less document delivered by the alarm in the seam's plane, as G103) is a section of the control-plane seam file beside A and A2, red by name until acquisition and screens-intake have merged.
(4) Platform check **P**: `op=heldat&address=<2021>` as the member, to read `copy.title` and settle that the stored title is BUDGET-2021-APP.pdf; **N** otherwise.
**Fix shape.** The row and the finished line read `copy.title` first and the chain last. **Old records:** none.

### G112 · After a reload the Sign step of an already prepared edition says "the signing check is not reached" and drops the set-time choice: the pre-flight is sent without `tieAttested` (B G62 NEW MINOR; diagnosis-R8-capture.md F)

**Rows.**
> **B.md:11** · G62 | `publishpreflight` with `tieAttested`, `reviewcopy`, and the Sign step for a prepared edition | 1280 | admin | new | `publishpreflight` POST {draft, project, tieAttested true, biasAcknowledgement} for DRAFT-2026-3094; `reviewcopy`; read the Sign step right after "Confirm your ties and prepare the edition" in both flows | FIXED (for the zone); NEW MINOR (after a reload) | `publishpreflight` with `tieAttested`: `ready: true`, `publish_at: {"offered":true,"zone":"America/Los_Angeles","source":"local-facts"}` (0.7 s, `publishpreflight;dur=503`); without `tieAttested`: `TIE_ATTESTATION_MISSING`, no `publish_at` (as the control). `reviewcopy`: `zone` America/Los_Angeles (0.43 s). Sign step right after preparing: "When it becomes public: Now, with "Sign and publish now"; or at a date and time in your team's time zone (America/Los_Angeles), with the other button", with Date, Time and a When select (Now / At a set time): not "isn't set" (rewalk6: said not set while Settings shows Pacific Time). NEW MINOR: reload the Ceremony of an already prepared edition (the bias box and the ties box come back empty): the Sign step then says "Whether this edition can be published at a set time is not known yet: the signing check is not reached, so only publishing now is described here" and offers only "Sign and publish now"; the member must tick ties and write the bias again to get the set-time choice back. A team with no zone: not exercised (one team only) | -
Owner **case-authoring** (R34/R75: a draft whose edition is prepared reads the signing check over the stored unsigned document, so the pre-flight needs no new attestation in the request); S control-plane (seam section F); screens-publish only if the job finds it must send something (R50 forbids sending an attestation the member did not make in this step). *Prov* ratification (`caseRatifyPreflight`, existing).
(1) **Cause, certain; reproduced (`probeF`).** After a reload the ceremony sends `publishpreflight` with no `tieAttested` (the Ties box is empty; R50 sends only what the member attested in this step). `publishPreflight` runs `publishCase` in preflight mode over those arguments, which stops first at `TIE_ATTESTATION_MISSING`; with no document text, ratification's signing check is not reached (`case-authoring/index.mjs:1599-1611`: `text === null ? {reached: false, why: ...}`), so the Sign step's `publish_at` is null with the "not reached" sentence (:1904-1906) and the screen draws it (`screens-publish/ceremony.mjs:372-385`, `why = "notreached"`). The ties were attested when the edition was prepared and the record holds that edition's unsigned document (`case_documents`, `editions.prepared`, :1760-1763); the pre-flight does not use it. The zone is known in the same read set (`reviewcopy` answers `zone`). G62's fix (round 7) offered the time right after preparing, where `tieAttested` is in the body, and left the reload path. With `tieAttested`: `publish_at {offered true, zone America/Los_Angeles, source local-facts}`; without it, `publish_at null` and `first` `TIE_ATTESTATION_MISSING`.
(2) **Missed:** G62's seam tests (`t51-round7-seam.test.mjs:480-500`) test "tied" (offered) and "not yet prepared" (not offered) and never "prepared, then pre-flighted as a reload does"; the ceremony screen test for G62 reads a pre-flight fixture with the answer hand-written.
(3) **Failing-first.** The owner's own (review2 G3): `case-authoring/r8-prepared-preflight.test.mjs`: with the real ratification (case-authoring uses it) and a prepared edition, `publishPreflight` with no `tieAttested` answers `publish_at {offered: true, zone}` and `first` is not `TIE_ATTESTATION_MISSING` (red: `publish_at` null, `PUBLISH_AT_NOT_REACHED_SENTENCE`, `case-authoring/index.mjs:1609-1611, 1904-1906`); control: an unprepared draft still answers `TIE_ATTESTATION_MISSING` and `publish_at: null`. Seam file section F then confirms through the door: "R8 (F): the Sign step of a prepared edition still offers a publishing time when the pre-flight is sent without `tieAttested`, as a reload sends it": with `profilesset oakland-alameda`, `preparedEdition(...)` as in the G62 test, `ceremonyPreflight(e, {tied: false})` answers a Sign step with `publish_at {offered: true, zone: "America/Los_Angeles"}` (red: null with "not reached") and `first` is not `TIE_ATTESTATION_MISSING` for a draft whose edition is prepared; control (green before and after): an UNprepared draft without `tieAttested` still answers `TIE_ATTESTATION_MISSING` and `publish_at: null`. Screen half, same file, in the manner of `ceremonySigned` (:753-763): `CE.open(CEREMONY, {plane: {publishpreflight: <the door's answer of the reload read>}})` over the prepared edition draws the date and time fields (`ce-date`, `ce-time`) and no `data-publish-at-none`.
(4) Platform check **L** (Miniflare, `probeF` done) and **P**: reload the prepared edition's Ceremony and read the Sign step.
**Fix shape.** A draft whose edition is prepared reads the signing check over the stored unsigned document; the "not reached" sentence stays for the real not-reached case. **Old records:** none.

### G113 · `op=interfacewords` answers all 25,808 interface words (6,347,975 bytes) at every sign-in, and the shell keeps none of them when no translation is held (B NEW MAJOR; diagnosis-R8-speed.md A)

**Rows.**
> **B.md:23** · (no cause id) | `interfacewords` read at every sign-in | both | admin | new | called `op=interfacewords` three times from a signed-in page; seen on every screen's load | NEW (MAJOR by the 5 s rule; the product's own share is small) | 200 with a 6,347,975-byte body (6.3 MB) each time: `interfacewords;dur=12197`, 11388, 13692 ms (wall 12.1-14.3 s); the door's own `do-interfacewords` is 155-202 ms and `do-session` about 110 ms, so most of the time is the 6.3 MB body through the proxy (a walker's note: the proxy's measured speed is about 8 MB/s on an upload, here about 0.5 MB/s down). On screens it appears as `Server-Timing` 8,286-13,127 ms on the first load of a session and does not block the screen from settling (Home settles at 5-6 s). A member on a slow connection pays 6.3 MB once per sign-in | -
Owner **translations** (the op's arm answers the shown words alone unless a language is asked; review G15: translations R8 and the op's declared description, "every interface word in a language ... never blank, with `en` beside it", `op-grades/t37.mjs:67`, `setup-words/rows.mjs:21294`, `translations/index.mjs:713-715`, are amended in the same job as BOB's edit of the requirement and the row, and the settings screen's `language` arm keeps "every word"); *prov* control-plane (G114, R116 amended), interface-shell (job 11, K4814 (1), review2 D1: its `loadWords` is the caller whose request the tests drive; for G113 itself its reading is unchanged because the shell already keeps only words with `fallback === false`, `browser.mjs:2652`, so an answer without the fallback words is read identically; its change is G114's `v`).
(1) **Cause, certain for what is in it and where the time sits; the transfer rate is not proven.** The answer is every word `{key, text, en, fallback}` (`translations/index.mjs:733-737`, `interfaceWords` :716). This copy holds no translation, so every word is `fallback: true` with `text` equal to `en`: the body is the English table twice (reproduced: 6,347,976 bytes, gzip 1,066,983, brotli 774,644). The shell reads none of it: `interface-shell/browser.mjs:2652` keeps only words with `fallback === false`. The object's answer arrives at once (`do-interfacewords` 155-202 ms to its headers); then the Worker reads the whole body in `tallySessionRefusal` (`control-plane/index.mjs:142-147`, `res.clone().json()`), `tallyOnTheWayOut` (:160-163, again) and, when `v` is named, `interfaceWordsCache` (`words-cache.mjs:27`: `arrayBuffer`, parse, SHA-256, gzip), before `withServerTiming` stamps the header (:882-886). Compute there is small (parse 18 ms, build and stringify 12 ms, the whole door 0.25-0.36 s in Miniflare), so the 11-13 s that `Server-Timing` counts are I/O waits between the object's headers and the end of its body: 6.3 MB / 12 s = 0.5 MB/s, the object-to-Worker leg (P19's Frankfurt object, the Worker elsewhere); that the leg is window-limited (about 64 KiB per 125 ms round trip) is an inference from arithmetic, not a measurement, because Miniflare has no latency. The settings screen alone needs the whole list (`screens-settings/translations.mjs:21` asks it by `language`). The fix does not depend on the rate: do not send the bytes. Reproduction at the platform's shape: done for bytes, compute and calls in `diagnosis-R8-speed.md` (A); the link cannot be shown locally.
(2) **Missed:** R116/P1 tested the cache headers and the `v` ordering on a body of the table's size in memory with no latency and no bytes-across-the-object assertion; nothing asserts what the shell USES of the answer against what it is sent; nothing counts the Worker's whole-body reads.
(3) **Failing-first.** `translations/r8-shown-words.test.mjs` (the owner's own failing-first at its interface, review G14: the default arm answers the shown words alone, the `language` arm every word; red first) and `bio-plane/test/m/control-plane/r8-words-door.test.mjs` (the door confirmation after translations merges; Miniflare, the real Worker, `wrap-entry.mjs`: count and size every Durable Object answer by route). A member signs in; the request is the one the real shell issues (drive `loadWords` in the shell harness with a page naming `cs-version`, capture its request); the body goes compressed through the real path (a request accepting gzip answers `content-encoding: gzip` and the same words once inflated, measured as bytes on the wire). (a) The object's `interfacewords` answer body is at most 4 KiB when no translation is held (red: 6,353,380 B), and the same through the door; (b) after one real `translationadopt` and `translationconfirm`, the answer holds exactly that word with `fallback: false` and the shell's `SHOWN` has it (green today and after: the control that the fix keeps the one use); (d) the settings translation screen's own request (`language` named, the full list wanted) still receives every word (control). Bytes and calls are asserted, never time (R-CLOCK). The cache half (c) is G114's, in the same file.
(4) Platform check **P** after the redeploy: the shell's own call on biosmoke8, `interfacewords;dur` under 0.5 s and a body under 4 KiB; **L** cannot show the link. **Logging that rides the deploy (K4428):** the door adds `body;dur=` (the time to read the object's answer body in the door) and `bytes=` to `Server-Timing`, which names the link if the 12 s ever recurs for another op; control-plane's phase clock accepts these module-stated entries (review G4) and a test asserts the entries exist, with their fields (R-LOG, review G3).
**Fix shape.** The arm answers the shown words alone (those the shell keeps) unless a `language` is named; the shell's reading of the answer is unchanged (its change is G114's `v`). **Old records:** none.

### G115 · The Queue's count cache moves on every request: its key counts every row the object writes, and every foreground request writes one (A G80 STILL MAJOR; diagnosis-R8-speed.md B)

**Rows.**
> **A.md:34** · G80 | Queue count, 12 calls at once | 1280 | member, admin | new | 12 and 24 concurrent `op=queue&count=true` | STILL (MAJOR) | member 12: batch 17.9 and 20.4 s; admin 12: 19.3, 19.6, 13.4 s; 24: 23.8 s. `do-queue` 0.75-1.9 s each (only 2 of 6 seen at 93-104 ms, the cache hits); one sequential count 1.9-2.6 s ST. Control: 12 concurrent `whoami` 3.7-4.2 s. No reading tick was running (`last_run` 77-141 s old) | -
Owner **record-core** ALONE (review D1, K4807 (3), K4811 (1), K4164: one owner): a counter of record writes with the bookkeeping tables excluded; amends R89/R90. **What record-core can count alone (review2 G1):** record-core does NOT hand every module its `sql`. `credentialsOf` builds `new Credentials({ sql: storage.sql, ... })` from the shared storage (`credentials/index.mjs:2036`), the ledger takes `ctx.storage.sql` (`plane/pending.mjs:85`), the store holds `this.sql = ctx.storage.sql` (`plane/store.mjs:172`), and record-core keeps its own private `#sql` (`record-core/index.mjs:370`, reached only inside the module through `sqlOf`, :355, used at :2014); `record-core/index.mjs:439` is `total_changes()` over the whole connection. So a hook record-core owns tallies by itself the statements of its own write path, and subtracts the rows of the bookkeeping tables (`sessions.last_active`, the door window, the alarm and ledger rows) only by a mechanism it owns: (i) triggers it creates on the bookkeeping tables it creates or finds at `writeCount()`'s first use, tallying their rows into a counter it subtracts, or (ii) taking `storage.sql` over so every module's statements pass its tally, which changes how credentials, the ledger and the store are built and is a CHANGE to them (P10). The job's FIRST act is a red test of the mechanism it chose (below); if no record-core-only mechanism works it returns to BOB and does not widen to three modules (K4811 (1)). No provider is planned: credentials, admission and plane keep only their other causes. Queue keys on `writeCount()` as built (`queue/index.mjs:189-191`). **Stated temporary state if G115 returns to BOB (review2 G1):** G116 goes ahead alone: its test (c) (one derivation per wave) and its unit hold, the sequential and staggered cases (a)/(b) wait for the counter, and live the count keeps recomputing after nearly every foreground request, as the walker measured (A.md:34). The derivation shared per signature is G116 (queue).
(1) **Cause, certain; reproduced on workerd through the real door with a real member session.** Round 7's cache key (`queue/index.mjs:189-191`: `record.writeCount() | own writes | 5 s bucket`) moves on every foreground request, because the key counts every row the object writes (`record-core/index.mjs:439`, `total_changes()`) and every foreground session read writes `sessions.last_active` (`credentials/index.mjs:565`, unless `background=1`) and every public op writes the door window (`admission` `doorwindow`: 3 writes for `groupidentity` and `bootstrap`). The walker's call carries no `background=1` (only the shell's own count does, `browser.mjs:433`), so each call bumps the counter before its own `queue` hop and the next call's signature differs. Measured: one foreground `whoami` or `queue&count=true` +1 write; a background count +0; `groupidentity` and `bootstrap` +3; 12 SEQUENTIAL foreground counts: 12 recomputes (the cache never hit); 12 staggered 8 ms apart: 5; 12 concurrent: 2; 12 concurrent background counts: 0; a screen's wave (6 foreground reads, then 2 background counts): 2 recomputes. Live, requests reach the object spread out by the proxy and a recompute takes 0.75-1.9 s, so most calls arrive with a moved counter: "2 of 6 hits" is the spread. Each tick also writes rows (units, ledger), so the count recomputes after every tick.
(2) **Missed:** `r7-count-cache.test.mjs` drives `queueFeed` with a SCRIPTED write counter (`state.writes`) or `withCounter: "real"` (documented, not used); nothing puts a session read, a door-window write or any request between two counts, and nothing runs two requests through the door. The invalidation tests (every producer's write moves the next count) are right and stay; the missing test is the converse: a write that is not the feed's must not move the key.
(3) **Failing-first** (review2 D3: no test imports a later module). (a) The owner's own (review D1/G14): `bio-plane/test/m/record-core/r8-bookkeeping.test.mjs` tests the hook at record-core's own interface (its own storage, a stand-in table named on the bookkeeping list, the module's real write path) and is the red test of the chosen mechanism (GAP 1): a write to a bookkeeping table leaves `writeCount()` as it was, a record write moves it (red: both move it). Counts only; no clock. The proof with the REAL credentials `touch` (`credentials/index.mjs:565`), the real admission `doorWindow` and the real scheduler and ledger writes is (b). (b) The through-the-door proof lives in the control-plane seam file (control-plane may import queue, credentials, admission, record-core and plane by path), section "R8 (G115/G116): the Queue count through the door", through `wrap-entry.mjs`, a real member via `memberadd`/`enroll`/`login`, counting recomputes by the `queue-count;desc=hit|miss` mark and the `rows-queue` entry that G116 adds to `Server-Timing` (observable at the door, so no `Probe` Store is needed): case 1, 12 sequential `op=queue&count=true` as the walker sends it: recomputes at most 1 (red: 12); case 2, the same 12 staggered 8 ms: at most 1 (red: 5); case 3, a screen wave: 6 foreground reads then the list and 2 background counts for the same viewer: at most 1 derivation in all (red: 2; G116's half) and the count equals the list's length; case 4, after each of a task resolve, a mute and a new notice the next count recomputes and is right (green before and after: the invalidation controls). The bucket (review2 G2, K4787, R-NOLOAD): `#countSignature` ends in `Math.floor(now / QUEUE_COUNT_BUCKET_MS)` (`queue/index.mjs:189-191`, 5 s, kept by G116) and the test cannot set the door's clock, so "at most 1" is asserted as at most 1 plus the number of 5 s boundaries the case's own wall crossed (the test takes its own start and end instants), or the case is stated through the `now` seam in a unit. The section merges after record-core and queue (it is control-plane's, 170) and stays red by name until both have merged.
(4) Platform check **L** done above (the counts) and **P** after the redeploy: R-QUIET, the walker's 12 and 24 concurrent counts and one sequential count on biosmoke8, the `queue-count` mark `hit` on the second to the last, and the `rows-queue` figure of a miss recorded (G116).
**Fix shape (ruled, K4807 (3), K4811 (1)).** The counter exclusion in record-core; the per-table version in queue is not built. **Old records:** none.

### G116 · One screen causes two or three full feed derivations of 0.75-1.9 s each: the list, the muted list and the count share nothing; why one live recompute costs 25-60 times the local one is unknown (A G80 STILL; diagnosis-R8-speed.md B and C)

**Rows.** The row quoted at G115 (A.md:34: "member 12: batch 17.9 and 20.4 s ... `do-queue` 0.75-1.9 s each"). Owner **queue** (R94: one derivation per (viewer, signature) serves the list, the muted list and the count); *prov* record-core (G115).
(1) **Cause, certain for the sharing; the live cost factor is the part not yet known.** The list is "made on every ask" (`queue/index.mjs`, pinned by `r7-count-cache.test.mjs` "control: the list ... never served from the count's keeping"), the muted list on Queue (S5) and the count are separate derivations, and the shell's wave of 15-25 foreground reads before them has already moved the key (G115), so Home and Queue recompute the count once per load plus the list plus the muted list: two or three derivations per screen, one after another on the one thread. In Miniflare with 300 documents and 120 open tasks (items 120, answer 188 KB) a count recompute is 30-40 ms and the list 33-36 ms; one admin count issues 47 distinct statements, 65 executions, the same with 30 or 120 documents, and scans only small tables. Live items are 46 (member) to 107 (administrator): at 0.3 ms an item that is 15-35 ms, so the live recompute is 25-60 times the local one. The live record (535,983 text units, 1,794 files, 496 documents, the walkers' notices) is not reproducible here. Reproduction tried: the two seeds, the statement and plan census (`plan.mjs`, `plan2.mjs`) and the workerd run, recorded exactly in `diagnosis-R8-speed.md` (B). **Logging that rides the deploy (K4428), R-CLOCK-proof:** per recompute the producers' `rows_read` and `rows_written` (the SQL cursor's own counters) and the item count by producer, written to the `Server-Timing` of `op=queue` (`rows-queue;dur=`) and one log line, and a hit-or-miss mark (`queue-count;desc=hit|miss`).
(2) **Missed:** as G115: nothing runs two requests through the door, and the walker's baseline of one sequential count (1.9-2.6 s) already includes the recompute, so "with and without" differed by an amount no test modelled.
(3) **Failing-first.** The seam file's count section, case 3 (G115 (3) (b), review2 D3): one derivation in all for a screen's wave (asserted with the bucket allowance of G115 (3), review2 G2; with G115 returned to BOB, case 3 and the unit hold and cases 1-2 wait, review2 G1); and `queue/r8-derive-once.test.mjs` (a unit with a stand-in producers' read, queue's own): the list, the muted list and the count of one viewer at one signature are served by one derivation (a counter at the producers' read: red 3, after 1), the list differs from the count only by slicing; the `rows-queue` and hit marks exist (red: absent).
(4) Platform check **L** done (the counts) and **P**: the `rows-queue` figure of a miss on biosmoke8 names the cost factor (rows read against items), R-QUIET.
**Fix shape.** One derivation per (viewer, signature) serves the three; the cache holds it for the 5 s bucket (same counter and bucket); the list is no longer made on every ask. This reverses a pinned test (review G16): `r7-count-cache.test.mjs` "control: the list ... never served from the count's keeping" (and R94) is rewritten to the new rule, and a control is added that a list a to-do write has just changed is not served stale. The entries `rows-queue;dur` and `queue-count;desc=hit|miss` are stated by queue and carried by control-plane's phase clock (review G4), each with a test (R-LOG, review G3). **Old records:** none.

**Section 0 of the plan (binding):**

## 0. Rules for every START (round 8 section 0 holds; carried)

- **R-FOUR** (K4426): each cause has (1) root cause with file:line, or "Cause unknown" with the diagnosis file that records the reproduction tried (its logging rides the one redeploy, K4428); (2) why the tests missed it and that gap fixed; (3) a failing-first test on the walker's or the corpus's exact case through the real entry path, seen red first (a case that names a standard-sized file runs in the job at the smallest size that shows the cause, its bound injected or its size scaled, and at full size in the corpus R set of section 7: rB D1); (4) the platform check **L** (local workerd or a capped child at the real limits), **P** (read-only probe of biosmoke8 through the proxy), **N** (none).
- **R-SEAM / R-ONE** (K4427): one owning job per cause with its providers; a through-the-door test uses the real merged providers and inputs made by the member's own acts (the E4 lesson); the walker's or corpus row is copied verbatim into the START. A seam test whose provider is unmerged is written first and stays red by name. The door tests live in ONE file, `bio-plane/test/m/control-plane/t51-round9-seam.test.mjs` (the pattern of `t51-round8-seam.test.mjs`), written by control-plane's two sessions (jobs 38 and 43); section 3b lists every section of it with its providers, its writer and the merge at which it turns green (rB D7); counting-door tests reuse round 8's `wrap-entry.mjs`. A provider's interface that others code against before it merges is specified in section 2c (rB D2); the job that uses it tests against the stub or the interface text, never against code not yet merged.
- **R-PATH** (new, from the four family reports): a cause is proved on the path the corpus names AND on its sibling paths: upload (one part and multipart), `acquire`, the doorbell pull, the archive member, the no-cursor door read, the cursor read. "Fixed on the path the test used" is the commonest miss (pdf P5, sheets P5, docs P5); every bullet below names the entry path of its red test, and the job adds the siblings it shares code with.
- **R-NOTMET / R-OLD / R-LIVE** (K4427, K4428): no "not met" holds a cause; stored-data fixes say "new data only" or carry a repair; platform causes start with a measurement; one deployment after every job is merged and BOB's check of K4426's four points, then a targeted re-walk.
- **R-STOP.** A "reading follows" test asserts the words are in `text_units` and the LAST line is searchable by `op=search`, never only that a reading exists.
- **R-CLOCK.** Workerd freezes `Date.now()` in synchronous work: tests assert counts (bytes pulled, calls, rows, yields, objects walked), never a time from the object. A wall time is taken by the test outside the object and only where a bullet says so (K4787, X30 is a cause here). Every bound a test waits on is an injected constant (the scheduler's budget, the scanner call bound: 50 ms in the test), and each bullet that named a wall bar names the count that replaces it or the same-run control it is compared with (rB G2); a real-alarm Miniflare test with a 40 s scanner is the slowest test of the suite and runs alone, five runs (R-NOLOAD).
- **R-PROXY** (K4733): live reads only through the environment's proxy, unchanged; the direct route is refused and never tried.
- **R-QUIET.** The PERFORMANCE re-time runs on a quiet copy; a time taken while a reading tick runs is reported as that.
- **R-NOLOAD** (K4787): every heavy file named below runs alone, five runs, and the time-sensitive set also on one core (`taskset -c 0`); the corpus R set (section 7) runs whole in its own session and container.
- **R-LOG** (K4428): the logging a cause plans is itself tested (the line exists, carries the named fields, is written BEFORE the slow await as well as after) and is a count, an instant or an identifier, never a secret.
- **R-SILENT** (K4571, K4630, K4831 (3)): a new bound is stated to the member in the same change, with the file's size and the limit, and never says "read" of a file that is not; a bound that changes what a member may do is Bob's (K1881) and no job raises or lowers one on its own. The standard's figures (K4628) are the bounds; reaching them is Stage 3 (K4834).
- **R-GATE** (new): an entry that depends on a Stage 0 measurement (GAP-0.1 / GAP-0.2) is marked **GATED** below. Its job starts with the non-gated part. The mechanism (rB G13): the figure is a named entry of `PLATFORM_FIGURES`, exported by `runtime-limits` (section 2c), each with `{value, source}`: `source` is today's ruling (for example "K4616 50 MB") until BOB records the P-M row in `PLATFORM.md`, and in the same commit BOB edits the entry to the row's value and `source: "PLATFORM.md P-M<n>"`; a test pins that no entry is changed without a `source` naming a row, and that an entry still citing its ruling holds that ruling's figure. Code never reads the Markdown. No job guesses a platform figure. The protective figures that BOB cannot supply before the STARTs (the pixels a page, the entries of a zip, the retained objects, a page's decoded content) are proposed constants the job uses with a comment "proposed; BOB records" (rB G8; section 2b), and the job reports what its capped child showed.
- **R-LAYER** (new): a module quotes only a module of a lower layer or lower order (`modules.json`); a lower module reaches a higher one's service only through a callback the composition root injects (the pattern of `owe`, `keepLater`, `pendSha`). The two places GAP's wording crosses this (READ_BOUNDS, the registrar) are ruled (K4835 (1), (2)) and their interfaces are specified in section 2c.
- **R-WORDS** (K3930, K4269; rB G1): every sentence a member reads is a stand-in keyed through `localize`, in the module that says it, and its row is owed to `later-ux.md` (section 5, one HANDOFF to UX-DESIGN); the `words` check fails a job that hard-codes text. The member-facing statements this plan adds are listed in section 5 and each START copies the rows for its job. A words row in `setup-words/rows.mjs` (G135d) is BOB's edit with the regenerate step (section 6), since `setup-words` has no job.
- **Ruled (K4835), carried here as ruled and not as recommended:** (1) `READ_BOUNDS` lives in `runtime-limits` (layer 1); GAP-1.2's owner is runtime-limits; every quoting module takes a `uses` edge to it. (2) GAP-1.4's job row in the filing transaction is written through a registrar callback injected into acquisition, capture and doorbell, never an import upward. (3) S22's test script is `pdf-worker`'s. (4) X22 includes `site-profiles`' `client-rendered.mjs`. (5) An empty-password scan PDF is stated, per GAP section 7.2 (N24), until GAP-3.2. (6) The harness's code is test-support's and plane's tests; plane-1 (test-only) merges first. **K4836** adds one cause, G143.

**Section 2c of the plan, the interfaces you provide or use:**

## 2c. The interfaces providers give (rB D2, D3, D4; rA G2, G6)

Providers merge in `modules.json` order, so a user coded before its provider merges needs the provider's interface in text. These are BOB's details (P17), recorded at adoption and copied into every START that provides or uses one. A user job tests against the stub or a minimal local implementation of the contract named here.

1. **The registrar, `registerJob`.** Wired by the composition root, **plane** (plane-2, job 37), in `plane/store.mjs` beside `capture.keepLater = (a) => pending.keepLater(a)` (`store.mjs:434-438`), as `capture.registerJob = (e) => pending.registerJob(e)`; acquisition, capture and doorbell read it as they read `keepLater` (`typeof cap.registerJob === "function"`, else `opts.registerJob`, else the caller proceeds without a row and says so in its answer, never silently); extraction receives it as `opts.registerJob` and its `owe` is `registerJob` bound to a kind. Signature: `registerJob({kind, sha, session?, cursor?, owed?, carrier?, parts?, detail?, verdict?, priority?}) -> {ok:true, existed:boolean, state} | {ok:false, reason}`; **synchronous**, never throws. `kind` is one of `keep`, `reading`, `ocr`, `subresources`, `unpack`, `fetch` (today's `PENDING_KINDS`, `plane/pending.mjs`) and `scan`, `safe_view`, `grade`, `ingest`; `sha` is the WHOLE digest (64 lowercase hex), except `fetch` and `subresources`, keyed by capture `session` as today (`SESSION_KEYED`). Idempotence key `(sha or session, kind)`; a second registration re-arms, updates `cursor`, `owed`, `detail`, `parts` only when given, never resets the counters; `priority: "raised"` is "Read it". **The transaction rule:** `registerJob` is a synchronous write on the object's own SQL storage, so a caller makes it inside the synchronous function it gives `core.transact(fn)` (`capture/index.mjs:462-464`, `doorbell/index.mjs:149-151`), and the row commits or rolls back with the filing; arming the alarm is a separate async `cap.armJobs()` (`schedulerOf(ctx, env).arm(now)`) after the commit. A caller whose `registerJob` returns `{ok:false}` throws inside its transaction (the filing rolls back) or answers a stated refusal, never swallows it (G98, G126c). **The job row** is plane-2's table, additive to `keeps_pending` (`KEEPS_PENDING_DDL`, `plane/pending.mjs`): the existing columns plus `state` (`queued`, `fetching`, `reading`, `partial`, `refused`, `finished`, `final`), `verdict` (JSON `{ok, size, limit, bound_key}`: the plan step's bound verdict), `cursor` (JSON), `written`, `skipped`, `owed`, `failed` (integers) with `failed_why` (JSON by cause), `last_progress_at`, `rate_per_s`, `carrier` (text: the R2 key or `snapshots/<name>`), `reader_version`, `priority`; per-unit attempt stamps in `job_unit_attempts(sha, kind, unit, tries, stamped_at)`; batch idempotence in `job_batches(sha, kind, batch, landed_at)`. The row is read by control-plane's `op=reading` and `owedwork`, by extraction's `completeness` and by the screens. **Stub:** test-support's `jobRegistrarStub()` implements the contract in memory (`registerJob`, `armJobs`, `rows()`, `armed()`), so jobs 23, 24, 25 and 28 test "a kill between the put and the transaction finds one job row" before plane-2; the same test with the real table is control-plane's seam, section 11 of section 3b.

2. **`READ_BOUNDS` and the ledger** (runtime-limits, job 2; K4835 (1)). Files `bio-plane/src/read-bounds.mjs` and `bio-plane/src/byte-ledger.mjs` (added to runtime-limits' `paths`, section 6). `READ_BOUNDS`: a frozen map `kind -> name -> {standard, alpha, protective, key}` (`key` names the member sentence's `localize` key, which the quoting module owns); `boundOf(kind, name)` returns the ACTIVE figure (the alpha, except the light kinds, which take the standard: K4834); `statement(kind, name, size) -> {size, bound, key}`. A quoting module writes `export const X_MAX = boundOf("pdf", "bytes")` with no numeric literal for a ceiling it keeps. `byteLedger`: `hold(n, tag)`, `release(n, tag)`, `peak()`, `held()` for backing stores; `capFor(N)` is the one cap convention; `PLATFORM_FIGURES` (R-GATE).

3. **`readRange(store, sha, offset, length)`** (acquisition, job 23) `-> Promise<Uint8Array | {ok:false, reason}>`; `sha` is the whole digest only; the capture is read from the whole object when it is held whole, else across the parts named by **`partsOf(sha)`** (provenance, job 21) `-> [{sha256, bytes}] | null`, a function of the whole's bytes and independent of the bundle. Used by extraction, file-safety, content and retrieval in place of resolving parts.

4. **`promote`'s register** (provenance, job 21; builder: screens-intake, job 35). `register` rows are wholes; a multipart document's row carries `parts: [{sha256, path, bytes}]`; a part digest is never a `register.capture_sha`; `homes` is computed over wholes only; `CAPTURE_HELD_BY_ANOTHER_BUNDLE` answers `{capture_sha, kind: "whole", holder}`. **Legacy input** (a part sent as its own register row, the shape until screens-intake's change merges and in any cached page): a register row whose digest is a file's `blobSha` and whose `path` matches `snapshots/<name>.partNNN` (`acquisition/index.mjs:1778`) is read as a part of the whole row named `<name>` and goes to the child table `register_parts`, never to `register`.

5. **`decodeText(bytes, {declared, name, kind})`** (ooxml, job 3) `-> {ok:true, text, encoding, assumed:boolean, replaced:number, replaced_ratio:number, disagree:number[]} | {ok:false, why:"ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED", name}`; UTF-8 (BOM or not), UTF-16 with or without BOM, the declared or detected legacy charset (windows-1252, ISO-8859-x); `assumed` is true when no declaration or BOM decided it; `disagree` lists the window offsets whose detection differs from the file's.

6. **`alarmDouble(opts)`** (test-support, job 1; no plane import) `-> {storage, getAlarm(), setAlarm(t), deleteAlarm(), run(fn), retries}`: `getAlarm()` answers null while a `run` is in flight, a spent alarm stays set until deleted, a failed `run` retries up to 6 times. Used by scheduler's `r8-budget` and `r8-stale` and by plane's harness; scheduler takes `uses: test-support` (section 6).

7. **The reader contract** (reading-pipeline-1, job 27; GAP-1.8): `plan(source) -> {kind, pages|rows, verdict, mode}` and `read(source, cursor, budget) -> {units, next, more, final?, counters:{written, skipped, owed, failed}}` over `source = {size, readRange(offset, length)}`; a whole read is a one-piece range; `next > cursor || final` is asserted by the wrapper (G129).

**Your entries, full text (section 2 of the plan, whole entries; the pieces that are yours are those of your row and of section 3a above, the rest of an entry is context for the jobs that carry it; the walker rows and the corpus recipes are quoted verbatim after the entries):**

### G91 · One alarm firing awaits its consumers one after another with no bound: a slow consumer ahead of `pending-work` holds the reading's due tick (A G58/G70; diagnosis-R8-watchdog.md Findings 1 and 4)

Owner **scheduler** (job 33); *prov* file-safety (G92), plane (G93/G94); S control-plane (the founder's `owedwork` arm). **Rows:** `A.md:11`, `A.md:12`, `A.md:22` (copied verbatim from round 8 section 2, G91).
(1) **Cause, certain as a mechanism; reproduced on workerd.** `onAlarm` runs every due consumer in registry order and awaits each tick with no bound (`scheduler/index.mjs:589-600`); `pending-work` is not in `SCHEDULER_ORDER` (`scheduler/index.mjs:97-104`) and is appended after it in registration order (`scheduler/index.mjs:542-546`; registered at `plane/store.mjs:508`), so it runs last (rA G7, rA N1); while a firing runs `getAlarm()` is null (`scheduler/index.mjs:581, 721`), so `health()` answers `alarm_at: null` (:712-714) and a request that arms finds a past wake (`#reconcile` :662-663, `pending.mjs:91, 319-321`). **The part not yet known:** which consumer holds the live firings (no per-consumer timing exists; the G70 line is written after the heartbeat, `scheduler/index.mjs:612`, and is lost on long firings). Reproduction at the platform's shape and the telemetry: `diagnosis-R8-watchdog.md` ("Reproduced on workerd"). **Ruled (K4807 (2), K4811 (4)):** per-firing budget about 10 s; rotation (the next firing starts at the consumer after the last run); an in-flight set (a call outlasting the budget is not abandoned and not started twice); `pending-work` not first; the 40 s scanner variant carries the order assertions.
(2) **Missed:** the scheduler tests' storage double (`scheduler/fixture.mjs:6-26`) answers `getAlarm()` with the last value set and its consumers answer at once, so a firing has no length; `r100-upload.test.mjs:82` binds `FILE_SCANNER` as an instant stub; G70's own test is a logging test and its line was lost. Gap fixed by GAP-1.1's platform-shaped double (G123c), which is written by test-support (job 1) so that scheduler can use it without an edge to plane (rB D3).
(3) **Failing-first**, red on today's tree: `bio-plane/test/m/plane/r8-headofline.test.mjs` with `r8-headofline-entry.mjs`, written by plane-1 (job 20, rB D3: round 8 gave it no job; red by name until scheduler and file-safety merge, green at plane-2's merge) (Miniflare, real alarm, `FILE_SCANNER` a service binding recording scan/render start and end, a ~1 MB text uploaded through the real door `POST /api?op=captureupload`); with the 40 s scanner the first `pass` of the text precedes the first `scan-end` and every `pass` precedes the first `render-end`, the scan still completes after the handler returned (the real-alarm Miniflare test with the 40 s scanner is run alone, five runs; the budget is the injected constant everywhere else); with the 3 s scanner counts only; the founder's `op=owedwork` asked every 500 ms never answers `alarm_at` in the past. `bio-plane/test/m/scheduler/r8-budget.test.mjs`, written by the scheduler job with test-support's `alarmDouble` and the budget injected as 50 ms (gated consumers `scanlike` before `reading`): `reading` ticks before the gate opens; `scanlike` not started twice; the per-consumer line (name, started, ended, outcome) written before and after each await over the injected threshold (R-LOG). Seam file section G91 through the door. Counts and order only (R-CLOCK).
(4) Platform check **L** (the two tests at the real alarm with a 3 s and a 40 s scanner) and **P** (the `alarm` events and the new per-consumer line, any consumer over 10 s, and a 12 MB text uploaded with a second file queued behind it, read after the redeploy). **Fix shape (BOB's, recorded at adoption):** budget 10 s, rotation, in-flight set, the heartbeat carries the last firing's slowest consumer. Old records: none.

### G92 · File-safety's scanner and renderer calls await a service binding with no bound: `#render`, `#providerScan` and `#makeCopy` one file after another inside the one firing (A G70; diagnosis-R8-watchdog.md Finding 1)

Owner **file-safety** (job 26); *prov* none. Rows as G91.
(1) **Cause, certain for the unbounded awaits; that they are the long consumer is probable and G91's per-consumer line names it.** `#scanner` awaits `s.fetch(...)` with no timeout (`file-safety/index.mjs:430-441`), `#render` the same (:973); `scanBatch` makes one batched call (:1090); the serial awaits are `renderBatch`'s loop (:1032), the routine-tool loop (:1106-1109) and `#makeCopy` (:1035); about twenty `#scanner` call sites, so the bound goes in `#scanner`. Reproduced in scratch with a 3 s `FILE_SCANNER` (`diagnosis-R8-watchdog.md`). Logging: each `#scanner` and `#render` call timed (name, digest prefix, started, ended, outcome), written before and after the await.
(2) **Missed:** no test makes the scanner slow; file-safety's tests assert what a scan decides, never how long the firing is held.
(3) **Failing-first:** `bio-plane/test/m/file-safety/r8-bounded.test.mjs`: a gated scanner stub; `scanBatch` of two queued files, the call bound injected as 50 ms (`callBoundMs`), returns with both still queued and the call in flight (red: awaits the gate; a count of calls made, not a time); no second call for the same batch; the result is recorded when the gate opens (a 40 s scan is not starved); gated renderer: `renderBatch` returns after the first with the second untouched, same for `#providerScan` and `#makeCopy`; the per-call line exists, written before the await.
(4) Platform check **L** (G91's test with both scanners) and **P** (the per-call line; files waiting scan at the end of the re-walk). **Fix shape:** 8 s a call from the firing's point of view, the call continuing and its result kept; the file stays queued, never opened or shown scanned, until its result is in. Old records: none. This job is file-safety's, which also owns G136 (one session).

### G95 · `stale` stays false through a stall made of completed firings (A G58/G70; diagnosis-R8-watchdog.md Finding 3)

Owner **scheduler** (`health`, job 33); *prov* plane (the route). Row `A.md:12`.
(1) **Cause, certain, by definition.** `health()` (`scheduler/index.mjs:709-716`): `since = max(owed, last_run)` and `stale = now - since > 2 * HEALTH_CYCLE_MS` (600 s); a completed firing resets the clock while the oldest wake has been owed for 691 s; a firing in progress is ignored. **Ruled (K4807 (2), K4811 (5)):** `stale` when work is owed, the oldest wake already come is at least 300 s old AND no firing has completed in 300 s, or one firing has run longer than 300 s.
(2) **Missed:** `spent.test.mjs:103-125` asserts the definition on a double; none confronts it with a stall of firings that complete.
(3) **Failing-first:** `bio-plane/test/m/scheduler/r8-stale.test.mjs`: walker numbers (`oldest_overdue` 691 s, no firing completed in 300 s, `running_since` null) `stale === true` (red); `running_since` 400 s old with a due row 400 s old true (red); controls false: row owed 60 s with a firing 30 s ago; nothing owed; a row in back-off; a healthy backlog of 30 rows with a firing every 10 s; one row owed 400 s with the last firing 20 s ago. (1) `capture/index.mjs:682-858`; the clock arm defers a reading that is already final; an upload's reading lives under `upload:<sha>`, not `capture.sha256`; fail-open file safety served captures whose home it could not find (F3a). Digest of a multipart upload is the whole's (hashed as it arrives). (2) Missed: multipart tests used a single digest source. (3) Red: SHEET C02 / SCAN E7 (a multipart TIFF and JPEG over 8 MiB): the document digest equals the whole's, one reading under it, the sentence names the type. (4) **L**.
- **G127c · Two distinct PDFs that share an 8 MiB part were refused `CAPTURE_HELD_BY_ANOTHER_BUNDLE` for the second member: part digests are register rows in the document key space (K4833 (4); `diagnosis-R9-capture-identity.md`, rA D6).** Owner provenance (job 21: the fix is G127a's, no second mechanism); screens-intake (job 35: the register builder); control-plane (the seam section). (1) **Cause, certain; reproduced three times under Miniflare with the real Worker, object and R2.** `screens-intake/capture.mjs:190` builds the promote `register` as the whole (`:189`) plus one row per part, from `acquisition/index.mjs:1778` (parts cut by the 8 MiB `PART`, `acquisition/unpack.mjs:162`); `provenance/schema.mjs:13` makes `register.capture_sha` the PRIMARY KEY, so a part is a row in the key space of a document; `provenance/index.mjs:1719` `shas` = every `regs` entry, parts included, plus `wholesOfFiles`, and `:1721-1729` refuses when another existing bundle holds a row for any of them. Two 10 MiB PDFs of same-length page words and the same filler stream (the kit's `pdfOf`, `r8-kit.mjs:90`) have different wholes and different part 1, and a byte-equal PART 2 (bytes 8 MiB to the end, which same-length words leave unmoved); the second member's part 2 row finds the first bundle as its home: `heldat` false, an owed `fetch` row `final`, `why CAPTURE_HELD_BY_ANOTHER_BUNDLE`, `detail {}`. Control: words 8 bytes longer shift the filler, part 2 differs, both held. Not H2 (no whole equals a part), not H3 (no subresource or receipt in the list). Were the refusal only relaxed, `#project` (`:629-646`) upserts `bundle_id=excluded.bundle_id` and MOVES the first bundle's part row, so `:1729` is not relaxed alone. Files under 8 MiB have one part equal to the whole and cannot show it; a 3 MB pair never reaches the alarm path. Logging that rides the redeploy: the refusal's detail carries `kind: whole|part` and the owed row carries the refused sha and the holder (plane-2's job row, `failed_why`). (2) Missed: the fixtures held one document per digest; `r8-seam-capture.mjs:42-45` records that its author HIT this refusal ("found at the first run") and worked around it by randomising 20000 filler bytes per file in `served`, so the red was designed away; `pdfOf` shares its filler across files of a size, so only tests that varied the word length passed, by luck; no provenance test promotes two bundles with one equal part (G127a notes: "no fixture shares a part"); the refusal reaches the member only as an owed `final` row with `detail {}` and nothing asserted on the second member's held state. Gap fixed by the three reds below, `served` no longer randomising for them, and the detail and sha above. (3) Red: (i) owner provenance, module interface: `promote` as bundle A with register [whole A, part A1, shared part] then as bundle B with [whole B, part B1, shared part]: B is accepted, each bundle's `parts` rows still read under their own bundle (no row moved), and `CAPTURE_HELD_BY_ANOTHER_BUNDLE` only when B registers A's WHOLE sha (red today at `:1729`); (ii) the seam section "K4833" (control-plane's file, section 5 of section 3b), the scratch test of the diagnosis promoted: two members, two 10 MiB `pdfOf` of same-length words and shared filler (not randomised), both held, each reads its own document; the same file by two members is refused for the whole only; (iii) the screen path: `promoteDocument` (`screens-intake/capture.mjs`) of a multipart document whose part equals another bundle's part. The shrunk spec is `diagnosis-R9-capture-identity.repro.mjs`. (4) **L** (Miniflare, the real provenance; reproduced, the control passes) and **P** (two PDFs of 9 to 10 MB, same-length words and the same filler stream, uploaded as two members on biosmoke8, read-only afterwards; two small PDFs cannot show it).
- **G127d · No `readRange(store, sha, offset, length)` over the whole: every reader of a multipart capture resolves parts itself, and citation and safety lookups key on a part (rA G2).** Owner acquisition (job 23: `readRange`), provenance (job 21: `partsOf`); consumers extraction-1 (28), file-safety (26), content (29), retrieval (30). (1) `extraction/index.mjs:781, 976 (#wholeOfPart), 1448, 2035` find a document's whole from a part; `acquisition/index.mjs:1778` names parts `snapshots/<name>.partNNN` and `:483-489` composes a whole only up to `MULTIPART_KEEP_MAX` (`:373`), so past 32 MiB a capture exists only as parts (`acquisition/index.mjs:373` comment) and every reader of a large capture must know the parts; `provenance/index.mjs:630-646` registers them as keys. The whole sha is the only key everywhere; `readRange` (interface in section 2c) hides the parts. (2) Missed: every test used a fixture of one digest, so no reader was ever asked for a large capture by its whole. (3) Red: a 100 MB multipart upload (the lazy writer, G123d) has one sha, one job, and citation and safety lookups by that sha answer; `readRange(store, sha, 8 MiB - 10, 20)` across the part seam equals the bytes; file-safety grades it from `readRange`; extraction reads it without `#wholeOfPart`. (4) **L**.
- **G127e · The migration of part-keyed rows already in live records (GAP-1.5; rB D11).** Owner provenance (job 21); BOB schedules the live run (GAP-1.5). (1) Live records hold part rows in `register` beside the wholes (`provenance/index.mjs:630-646`; `provenance/schema.mjs:13`); a part row that is not the digest of any file marked as a whole is moved to `register_parts` under the whole that names it, counted, never losing a bundle's `capture` link; the pattern is `migrateRepairs` (`extraction/index.mjs:1982`). Old records (R-OLD): the migration is the repair. (2) Missed: no fixture held part rows in the old shape. (3) Red: the migration replays on a copy of a record holding part rows (built by the old register code kept as a test fixture): afterwards each bundle's parts read under it, `homes` over wholes only, the count recorded, a second run changes nothing. (4) **L** and **P** (the count of part rows before and after on the copy, read-only).
- **G127f · The presigned direct-to-R2 upload, only if the zone caps the body (GAP-1.5; GATED: P-M1, P-M3; rB D11).** Conditional job 42, opened by BOB only after `PLATFORM.md` records P-M1 as capped; not in the capture job and not planned further until then. (1) A platform fact not yet measured (Stage 0 P-M1, the team zone's body cap, and P-M3, presigned per-part signing); the code it would replace is the upload through the Worker, `capture/index.mjs:682-858` and `capture/ops.mjs:83-85`. ### G128 · GAP-1.6 Units land in batches; completeness is one fact (S4): owner extraction; prov record-core, retrieval, retrieval-browse, content, text-chain, reading-pipeline. **GATED** for the batch size (P-M9, GAP-0.2): starts with header row plus unit rows, batch-per-transaction and idempotence by (job, batch); the batch byte size is a named constant `LAND_BATCH_BYTES` (GAP: about 1 MB; never a row near the 2,098,176-byte value) set from `PLATFORM.md` when P-M9 is recorded

### G129 · GAP-1.7 Progress only forward; failures classified; the poison rule (S5): owner extraction; prov plane, scheduler, reading-pipeline, csv-reader (S-22's list), acquisition (X20)

Intro. The step wrapper asserts `next > cursor || terminal`; failures are deterministic, transient or resource; a per-unit attempt stamp is written before the reader runs; a unit that kills its reader three times is stated for that unit alone (`PAGE_KILLED_READER`) after one retry at half the budget; "three interruptions end the whole read" is retired (CF-18; DEC-417's sentence is a HANDOFF to UX-DESIGN). (2) Missed: R37's tests are first-tick; the plane counts deaths of the plane, not of the member. (3) Failing-first: store failure at tick 2; a page that kills its reader three times leaves the rest read; an encrypted ranged PDF; an image's OCR continued after a restart. (4) **L**.

- **G129a · An encrypted PDF read in pieces never finishes: `readRange` answers `count: 0`, `next = from + 0`, `more` stays true (G102 = N1).** (1) `openPdfRange().range()` returns `{count:0, ... marker "encrypted"}` (`pdfstructure.mjs:4525-4529`); `rangePieceAnswer` computes `next = p.more ? ... : from + count` and `more = next < total` (`reading-pipeline/index.mjs:1548-1559`). Ruled (K4803, K4807 (4), K4831 (6)): a file needing a user password ends in one tick `final`, "needs a password" (`PASSWORD_REQUIRED`), never "being read" after; the guard is generic (any piece that advances nothing ends the entry stated). The empty-password file: its text pages are read (tier 2 decrypts); its scanned pages are STATED unread in the alpha (N24 is Stage 3, GAP 7.2) — this narrows round 8 G102's test, which expected the whole file read; the corpus case C11 is judged on text pages read and scans stated. (2) Missed: every piece test uses the builders' own unencrypted PDF; nothing states every tick advances. (3) Red: `bio-plane/test/m/reading-pipeline/r8-liveness.test.mjs` through the ledger's ticks (`shape/harness.mjs`): an RC4-128 empty-password file (5 text pages, 2 scans) and an AES-128 user-password file (PDF C11, C14): six ticks never the same cursor twice; the user-password file ends in one tick; and a stub reader answering `count: 0` ends stated. (4) **L**. Old records: stuck entries end at the first tick after the fix; P counts reading rows older than a day.
- **G129b · At tick 2 or later a transient failure comes back with no `more`, `#continueText` writes `more=0` and ends the read, never retried (N14 = S20 = X13 = S-7).** (1) `extraction/index.mjs:881-885`; `reading-pipeline/index.mjs:2263-2268, 2369, 2434, 2720-2724`; `pending.mjs:179`; one R2 hiccup leaves the file indexed to `read_through`, `partial`, no reason. A throw on the file's own bytes is deterministic (final with its reason), not `transient` (X12, G133c). (2) Missed: R37's tests are first-tick; the scripted provider in `round6.test.mjs` was not read to a later tick. (3) Red: PDF E7, SCAN E2, SHEET (J-8), DOCS F1 (`storeFailAt` tick 3 / 2 on T1, H2, O1, P1, A10): retried and whole, or `final` with its reason, never `partial` without one. (4) **L**.
- **G129c · A kill is retried without end, three interruptions end the whole read, the plane counts a member kill `transient` (E6; N12/N23 consequence).** (1) `plane/pending.mjs:63 (PENDING_INTERRUPTED_MAX = 3), :193-202`; the member's 5xx counted transient (`reading-pipeline/index.mjs:619-652`). A per-unit attempt stamp before the reader runs; after three kills of one unit that unit is `PAGE_KILLED_READER`, one retry at half budget, the read goes on; a poison DOCX no longer ends the row "the object stopped". (2) Missed: no `killPage` fault. (3) Red: PDF E10 / SCAN D12 (`killPage` 150 of a 300-page scan: page 150 stated after 3 kills, the rest read) and DOCS `killAt`. (4) **L**.
- **G129d · No OCR continuation for a standalone image (S4).** (1) `#continueOcr` calls the PDF-only `pdfStructure` (`extraction/index.mjs:903-917, 2198-2203`); `readHeld(kind:"ocr")` answers `UNREADABLE` with the member up and 0 calls; the ledger ends the row finished (`plane/pending.mjs:206-207`); `op=pdfstructure&ocr=1` on a JPEG is 422 `NOT_A_PDF`. The OCR remainder of an image is a job like a PDF's. (2) Missed: no transient OCR failure on a picture. (3) Red: SCAN E3 (A1 uploaded, the OCR down for three ticks then up: retried to the end, the row does not end at `UNREADABLE`). (4) **L**.
- **G129e · A transient store error during unpack marks a member `not_filed` for good; continuation members and nested archives' members land in no project (X20).** (1) `acquisition/unpack.mjs:701-713` (also :162-195, 522, 606-713); `plane/unpack.mjs:83`; `pending.mjs:261`; `control-plane/archive.mjs:5-8`. ### G133 · GAP-1.8 (c), office and ODF readers made correct within the alpha: owner office-readers (docx, pptx, xlsx), odf-reader and ooxml own their bullets; prov doc-clean (G136), reading-pipeline

Intro. ODS gets held cells and row windows and merged ODS cells keep their columns; office text readers stop throwing on an out-of-range character reference and have work bounds against quadratic scans; the ZIP central directory is read once in physical order with an entries limit; classification is a budgeted step. (2) Missed: no office file was ever run through the plane's harness; the builders make well-formed UTF-8 text-heavy files (docs P1, P4). (3) Failing-first: DOCS and SHEET corpus files through upload and the ticks. (4) **L**.

- **G133a · An ODS cannot be read within the bound: odf-reader has no held-cells contract, so a 20.4 MB `content.xml` dies at the object's heap and over 20 MiB is refused (G99 = S-2; S-1 for ODS).** (1) `odf.mjs` has no `inlineCellsMax`/`cellsIn`/`cells_held` (`odf.mjs:2202` ignores `inlineCellsMax`; `odf.mjs:674-700, 679-690`, R35/R48); 172,302 cells, a 29.7 MB reading, a 142 MB heap uncapped, dying at 112 MB (`diagnosis-R8-reading.md` B (1)(a)). Row `A.md:37`. Fix: the held-cells contract of csv-reader R17/R18 (`text(parts, {inlineCellsMax, yieldNow})`, `cells: null` and `cells_held: true` past the opt-in, `cellsIn(parts, {sheet, fromRow, toRow, ...})`), `content.xml` read as a stream of rows in windows; the 20 MiB guard STAYS (K4831 (3), R-SILENT; rA D9): the job does not raise it, and if the capped child shows the held-cells path reads more, the figure change is BOB's to report to Bob (K1881), not the job's. (2) Missed: `odf-reader/large.test.mjs` tests the over-bound refusal; `g41-held.test.mjs` was xlsx and CSV only. (3) Red: `odf-reader/r8-held-cells.test.mjs` in a child at `--max-old-space-size=112` on a 20,400,000 B `content.xml` (`sheets[0].cells === null`, `cells_held`, the whole sheet text, peak under the bound; `cellsIn` rows 70000-70010 equal the whole listing's); door "R9 (B, plane 2b): a 19.5 MiB ODS": `op=reading` whole, the LAST row in `text_units`, `op=cells` rows 1..50; SHEET O03-O06 (a file over the stated bound, 20 MiB, is refused with its figure; the capped child's finding is reported, the bound unchanged). (4) **L** and **P** (the ODS digest's `owedwork`, `op=reading` raw, `heldcaptures`, `captureupload` Server-Timing). Old records: stored ODS readings stated "too large" are re-read by an extraction migration (`too_large.key == cells`, container ODF), counted at P.
- **G133b · ODS `table:covered-table-cell` is never iterated, so every cell right of a merge shifts one column left; wrong citations (S-18).** (1) `odf.mjs:1507` (B ran: a cell at C1 reads as B1). XLSX merged ranges are unread and unstated (a stated gap, parity case in Stage 3). (2) Missed: no merged cell fixture. (3) Red: SHEET O09 (merged cells with values to the right keep their columns), X14 stated. (4) **L**.
- **G133c · "Never throws" is false: `&#x110000;` in `core.xml`, a rels target or `[Content_Types].xml` throws `RangeError`; a lone-surrogate rels target throws `URIError`; a deterministic throw is `transient` and retried for ever (X12 = S-21 first part).** (1) `ooxml.mjs:722-730` (no range test, unlike `docx.mjs:177`, `pptx.mjs:181`); `subresources.mjs:810` (`encodeURIComponent`); the pipeline records the throw as `FAILURE_TRANSIENT` (`reading-pipeline/index.mjs:2720-2724`); `meeting-calendar.mjs:151` (`Math.min(...dates)` throws at 150,000 dates, doctypes). A throw on the file's own bytes is `final` with its reason. (2) Missed: no hostile character reference. (3) Red: DOCS O7 and SHEET X17 (`&#x110000;`, `&#99999999999;`, `&#xD800;`, `&#0;`, `&#12ab;` in `core.xml`, a rels target, `[Content_Types].xml` and the body): never throws, deterministic failure `final` with its reason. (4) **L**.
- **G133d · Quadratic scans on tiny input: a 953-byte DOCX takes 8.0 s, a 400 KB unclosed `<w:comment ` 20 s, ODF `<a ` 9.7 s, `doc-clean/xml.mjs:130` 21 s (X6).** (1) `docx.mjs:196, 425, 803-805` (`TOKEN_RE`); `pptx.mjs:197`; `odf.mjs:359, 1195-1212, 1954-2013`; `doc-clean/xml.mjs:130` (G136d). A linear scanner (no backtracking attribute group), a tag at most 64 KiB, the walk counts bytes scanned per tick; yields every 64 Ki tokens (the office entries have none: `entry.text` takes one parameter). (2) Missed: the object cannot time itself (R-CLOCK); no work count. (3) Red: counts of bytes scanned at most 4 times the part for DOCS O7/P6/D4 (unterminated tag, 40,000 copies of `<a b="`, 20,000 unclosed comments, `<!x>` x 200,000). (4) **L**. Dropped at Stage 3.
- **G133e · Archive listing is unbounded and repeated: a shuffled central directory of 10,000 entries costs 9,180 part reads and 106 s, physical order 25 reads (X4).** (1) `ooxml.mjs:1537, 1563-1572, 1604`; `acquisition/unpack.mjs:125-158, 259-272, 578-606` (entry rows keep neither method, CRC nor offsets, so each 100-entry tick lists again: 306 evidence operations per 100 small entries); 2,000 entries with 60,000-byte names list at 343 MiB. Parse the directory once in physical order; directory at most 16 MiB, a name at most 4,096 bytes. (2) Missed: central directories in file order only. (3) Red: DOCS A5, A6, A4 (10,000 entries shuffled: part reads at most 2 x the physical-order count, ticks do not list again; 10,001 refused whole by name). (4) **L**.
- **G133f · `doctypeFor` classification is unbounded in one piece: 6.9 s at 12 MB, 14-16.6 s at 24 MiB, a 12 MB DOCX/HTML classified whole through `readText` (X25; X22's quadratic patterns are G140).** (1) `reading-pipeline/index.mjs:1918, 2418-2447, 2662`; `docprofile/doctypes/registry.mjs:85-112`; `readtext.mjs:263-265`; a 1 MB CSV makes 129 MB of garbage; `op=reading` of a finished 1,328-unit PDF reading kills the object. Classification is a budgeted step: head-and-sample above 2 MiB for every kind, each detect given a window and a work count. (2) Missed: yields only between detects and only for plain text (G88). (3) Red: DOCS T1/T4 and Q1 by count: classification work per tick bounded, the tick's longest stretch within the bound. (4) **L**.

**Corpus recipes, verbatim from the family reports (size, shape and the check of each named case; the first word names the family).** Reading the tables: PDF and SCAN rows are `# | File (how made) | Path | Passes when | Owner`, DOCS rows `ID | File, how generated | Path | Passes when | Owner`, SHEET rows `Id | The file | Today | Judged by`; `R` is the real path through the plane's harness, `M` the owner's interface, `L` the live subset, sizes are the file's bytes unless "declared". A case is built by the generators of test-support (`pdfgen`, `imggen`, `sheetgen`, `docgen`) with the family's fixed seed unless the row names one; the smallest case that shows the cause runs in your job (a scaled window or an injected bound, R-FOUR (3)) and the full size in the corpus R set at the round end (plan section 7); a generator option not yet in the merged library is built in your own test directory first, marked `// MOVE` (G123 intro). The J-n and I-n and SI-n invariants a row cites are quoted after the rows.

**Invariants of the PDF family (verbatim):**

### 2.4 The invariants (the "always", stated once; each corpus file is judged against all of them)

The invariants each name the module whose requirements carry them; BOB writes the wording into those requirements (§4).
- **I1 Every page once.** Each page in `[0, pages)` is read exactly once across the pieces, in order, and the units equal the generator's truth for that page.
  - Requirements: reading-pipeline R40, pdf-reader R43.
- **I2 Progress.** Every tick that answers `more: true` moves the cursor forward; the number of ticks is at most `pages` plus 2.
  - Requirements: reading-pipeline, plane R77.
- **I3 Never silent.** Every page that is not read whole carries a named reason in the reading and in the member's statement. `completeness` is "whole" only when the cursor is at the end, nothing is owed, and no page is unread for a reason other than "has no text".
  - Requirements: extraction R91, K4571.
- **I4 Memory.** The child running the real plane at 128 MB, with the held bytes inside it, stays alive, and its peak is within E4's budget.
  - Requirements: pdf-reader R41, reading-pipeline R36, superseded by E4.
- **I5 Work per tick.** Every tick's counted work is within E1's budget, and its longest synchronous stretch is within E2's (a count of operators between yields, not a time).
  - Requirements: reading-pipeline R40, plane R93.
- **I6 Pieces equal whole.** The stored reading of a PDF read in pieces equals its whole read, field for field, the fields of F2.
  - Requirements: reading-pipeline R40, R12.
- **I7 A refusal is stated, final and finite.** Every bound in §2.2 answers its named statement once. Nothing is owed forever, no kill is retried more than E6's 3 times, and no row stays "being read" after the last tick.
  - Requirements: plane R77, extraction R82.
- **I8 State loss is harmless.** Losing the open document, or restarting the object, between any two ticks gives the same reading and units; only the tick's work differs.
  - Requirement: reading-pipeline R40.
- **I9 Every path owes what it leaves.** Upload, promote, press, ledger, `op=pdfstructure` and `pagetranscribe` all owe pages and ranges a cap left.
  - Requirements: reading-pipeline R37, extraction.
- **I10 Search finds the last line.** The last line of the last page (and a first and a middle line) is found by `op=search` and is citable.
  - Requirements: extraction R22, R90.
- **I11 The reading pages to its end.** `op=reading` with `next` reaches the last page with no answer over 1 MiB; the Document screen's Next page reaches it.
  - Requirements: extraction R93, screens-inquiry.
- **I12 Stored values bounded.** No stored value exceeds 1 MiB, and none exceeds the platform's value bound.
  - Requirement: extraction R96.
- **I13 Contention.** While the corpus file's ticks run, cheap reads through the door wait under 500 ms at p95.
  - Requirement: plane R93.
- **I14 Same bytes, same answer.** Two runs of the same file give byte-identical readings and units.
  - Requirements: pdf-reader R24, reading-pipeline.

**Invariants of the SCAN family (verbatim):**

### 4.4 The invariants (the "always", stated once; each corpus file is judged against all of them)

The invariants name the module whose requirements carry them; BOB writes the wording into those requirements (§6). PDF #1's I1-I14 hold for scanned PDFs; these are the scan family's, with the ones it shares noted.
- **SI1 Every page once, and what is left is owed.** Every page of a scan is OCR'd exactly once or stated unread with a named reason; any page a cap leaves is owed on **every** path (upload, promote, press, ledger, `pagetranscribe`, `pdfstructure`); `ocr_left` is read by every caller. *(reading-pipeline R37, extraction R82; PDF #1's I9.)*
- **SI2 Never silent.** `completeness` is "whole" only when nothing is owed and no page is unread for a reason other than "has no text"; a type not read is named; a refusal's own reason reaches the member (S2, S3, S11). *(extraction R91, K4571.)*
- **SI3 Declared before allocated.** Every route judges the header's declared size before any allocation or inflate; a hostile header gets a named 200 refusal in under 1 s and the process stays flat (S5-S7). *(image-codecs, pdf-pixels, image-cover.)*
- **SI4 Memory.** The plane child at 128 MB with the held bytes inside it stays alive and within PDF #1's E4 budget; the OCR member's peak working set (C1) stays within its measured bound for every admitted frame. *(pdf-pixels, ocr-worker, plane.)*
- **SI5 Work.** No member request does OCR or a decode over its K4354 bar; every tick's OCR wait is within C3. *(control-plane, reading-pipeline, plane R93.)*
- **SI6 Progress and finiteness.** Every `ocr` tick that answers `owed` advances or is `final` and stated; a kill is an attempt; a page killed 3 times is stated; no row is "being read" after the last tick. *(plane R77, ocr-worker.)*
- **SI7 Search finds the last line.** A marker word on the last line of the last OCR'd page (and a first and a middle one) is found by `op=search` and is citable with its page. *(extraction R22, R90.)*
- **SI8 Truth.** A page that OCR reads contributes its words at the stated floor (≥ 90 percent on a clean page; the amended floor on a stated-hard one); a page below the legibility floor is stated and is not stored as text (S9, B-S6). *(ocr-worker R25.)*
- **SI9 State loss is harmless.** Restarting the object between any two ticks, or losing the open document, gives the same reading and units. *(reading-pipeline R40; PDF #1's I8.)*
- **SI10 Pieces equal whole.** The OCR text of a scan read in pieces equals the whole read's, and each piece's chain names its pages (S25). *(reading-pipeline R40.)*
- **SI11 Stored values bounded.** No stored value exceeds 1 MiB, and none exceeds the platform's value bound. *(extraction R96.)*
- **SI12 Same bytes, same answer.** Two runs of the same image give the same pixels (`pixels_sha256`) and the same units. *(pdf-pixels R40, image-codecs R8.)*
- **SI13 Honest acts.** No act is offered on a document that it cannot perform; a type not read says so by name once. *(screens-inquiry, extraction.)*
- **SI14 Contention.** While a scan's ticks run, cheap reads through the door wait under 500 ms at p95. *(plane R93; PDF #1's I13.)*
