**Invariants of the DOCS family (verbatim):**

### 4.4 The invariants (the "always", stated once; each corpus file is judged against all of them)

I1-I14 are the PDF plan's (every unit once; progress; never silent; memory; work per tick; pieces equal whole; a refusal is stated, final and finite; state loss is harmless; every path owes what it leaves; search finds the last line; the reading pages to its end; stored values bounded; contention; same bytes, same answer), read for *units* (paragraphs, slides, lines, members) instead of pages. The kinds here add:
- **I15 Declared is not allocated.** A bound is on what the read allocates and walks (objects, buffers, tokens), with ArrayBuffers counted; a file whose declared size passes and whose walk would not fit is refused *before* the walk, by name. Requirements: office-readers R12, ooxml R35 (rewritten), reading-pipeline R36.
- **I16 An encoding is named.** Every text read states the encoding it was decoded in; a decode that replaces more than the tolerance is partial with the count; nothing is `U+FFFD` and `failure: null`. Requirements: reading-pipeline R1, R32; docprofile R18.
- **I17 Every part is read or named.** A part of a file that is text is a unit; a part not read is named by kind and count in the reading and in the member's statement; the units of a document are never lost to a bound on something else (cells, links, comments). Requirements: office-readers R11; extraction R91, R97; K4571.
- **I18 Every member is accounted for.** An archive's members are each `filed`, `read`, `refused with a cause`, or `waiting for a named act`; none is `waiting` for ever; an uploaded archive is opened like a fetched one. Requirements: acquisition R38/R40.
- **I19 A copy says what it is.** A visitor view carries its fidelity ("first load, not scrolled"), a failed one carries its detail and its retry class, and a copy that did not render is not filed as the document. Requirements: capture-sources, acquisition R100.
- **I20 A deterministic failure is final, with its reason.** A throw on the file's own bytes, or a refusal by a bound, is never `transient`; a transient failure is retried under R77 until it ends or becomes final with its reason. Requirements: reading-pipeline R37; plane R77.
- **I21 A verdict that was not reached says so.** A file the scanner could not look inside (encrypted, over a limit, unrarred) is never `clean`; the member is told which. Requirements: file-scanner R3; file-safety R51.
- **I22 The statement names the cause.** The sentence for a refusal is the one for its cause, with the figure; "Read it" is offered only where it can succeed. Requirements: reading-pipeline R37; screens-inquiry; setup-words.

Corpus files are judged against I1-I22. Lines in §5 say which are particular to the file.

---

**Invariants of the SHEET family (verbatim):**

### 4.4 The invariants (the "always"; stated once; each corpus file is judged against all of them)

The PDF plan's I1-I14, re-cut for a sheet. Each is a sentence for the module named; **a counted bound, never a time** (R-CLOCK). The corpus and §5.3 refer to them as J-n.

- **J-1 Every row once.** Each data row of the file is in exactly one indexed unit; the header line is the *first line of a block* and never a data row; every unit's extent contains exactly the rows it holds. (reading-pipeline R41, new text)
- **J-2 Progress.** A read finishes in at most ⌈unit text ÷ 2 MiB⌉ + 2 ticks; no tick ends with the cursor unmoved; a failure of any tick is retried, never ends the read as finished. (reading-pipeline R40; plane R77)
- **J-3 Never silent.** `completeness` is `whole` only when every row of the file is in the index; anything cut, held, skipped, undecodable or refused is stated with its **size, its bound and its cause in the cause's own words** (not "undecodable region"). (reading-pipeline R15/R35; extraction R90/R91; K4571, K4630)
- **J-4 Memory.** A reader's retained heap between windows is at most 2× its window plus O(columns); the whole read stays alive in a child at the platform's cap with the held bytes inside it. One cap convention for every format: **the platform's measured idle, written once** (S-38).
- **J-5 Work per tick, by count.** A later tick hands an entry at most 5 MiB; it hands the store at most 5 MiB of ranged reads; every window is followed by a yield; no stored value exceeds 1 MiB. (reading-pipeline R40, csv-reader R20, office R35, odf R-new)
- **J-6 Pieces equal whole.** The cells and the text of a windowed read equal a whole read's, and both equal the generator's truth, field for field. (The oracle is the generator, never the reader's past output: P7.)
- **J-7 A refusal is stated, final and finite.** Past a bound the answer is final, names the size and the bound, and no row remains "being read" after the last tick.
- **J-8 State loss is harmless.** A restart or a store failure between ticks (after any tick) neither loses a row nor ends the read: the read resumes or re-reads, once.
- **J-9 Every path owes what it leaves.** Upload (one part and multipart), the doorbell pull, address `acquire`, the daemon paths, and the no-cursor door read all end `whole` or owed; none ends `whole` with `truncated`.
- **J-10 Search finds the first, a middle and the last row,** by a mark placed in a non-header cell; the hit's extent contains the row; `cellsOf` of that extent contains the cell with the mark equal to the generator's.
- **J-11 Cells keep their place.** The cell at A1-style address *x* of a file is *x*, with empty cells, merged cells (the covered cells keep their columns), rows without `r`, and a styled empty cell not moving a value or the used range; text and cell views agree.
- **J-12 Stored values bounded.** No stored value and no reading row over 1 MiB; **a reading is never replaced by a statement while its text could be indexed** (the ODS cliff); a reading over the guard still has its units.
- **J-13 One table, one sha.** A table declared from the same data, whichever kind (CSV comma or semicolon or tab, XLSX, ODS), whichever path (sheet range, document extent, passage, bytes), whichever window cut, has the same rows, the same columns and the same sha; the header appears once; an empty cell keeps its column; a quote, a tab or a line break in a value is a value.
- **J-14 Money by truth.** An ingest of a generated table writes the facts the truth names, each fact's source row is the sheet row; a percent, a currency mark, a locale separator and a date are read as the table declares them, or stated.
- **J-15 Same data, same answer, in every kind.** A workbook and its CSV, TSV and ODS twin give the same cells and the same searchable words, dates included.
- **J-16 Contention.** While sheet ticks run, twelve cheap reads wait under 500 ms at p95 on workerd (R93), **asserted** for the real reads, not printed.
- **J-17 Encodings.** Every cell whose characters the file's encoding can represent is read: no valid UTF-8 character is `null`; a windows-1252 file is decoded with the encoding stated; an undecodable cell is counted, listed up to 200, and never shifts a column.

## 0. Rules for every START (round 8 section 0 holds; carried)

- **R-FOUR** (K4426): each cause has (1) root cause with file:line, or "Cause unknown" with the diagnosis file that records the reproduction tried (its logging rides the one redeploy, K4428); (2) why the tests missed it and that gap fixed; (3) a failing-first test on the walker's or the corpus's exact case through the real entry path, seen red first (a case that names a standard-sized file runs in the job at the smallest size that shows the cause, its bound injected or its size scaled, and at full size in the corpus R set of section 7: rB D1); (4) the platform check **L** (local workerd or a capped child at the real limits), **P** (read-only probe of biosmoke8 through the proxy), **N** (none).
- **R-SEAM / R-ONE** (K4427): one owning job per cause with its providers; a through-the-door test uses the real merged providers and inputs made by the member's own acts (the E4 lesson); the walker's or corpus row is copied verbatim into the START. A seam test whose provider is unmerged is written first and stays red by name. The door tests live in ONE file, `bio-plane/test/m/control-plane/t51-round9-seam.test.mjs` (the pattern of `t51-round8-seam.test.mjs`), written by control-plane's two sessions (jobs 38 and 43); section 3b lists every section of it with its providers, its writer and the merge at which it turns green (rB D7); counting-door tests reuse round 8's `wrap-entry.mjs`. A provider's interface that others code against before it merges is specified in section 2c (rB D2); the job that uses it tests against the stub or the interface text, never against code not yet merged.
- **R-PATH** (new, from the four family reports): a cause is proved on the path the corpus names AND on its sibling paths: upload (one part and multipart), `acquire`, the doorbell pull, the archive member, the no-cursor door read, the cursor read. "Fixed on the path the test used" is the commonest miss (pdf P5, sheets P5, docs P5); every bullet below names the entry path of its red test, and the job adds the siblings it shares code with.
- **R-NOTMET / R-OLD / R-LIVE** (K4427, K4428): no "not met" holds a cause; stored-data fixes say "new data only" or carry a repair; platform causes start with a measurement; one deployment after every job is merged and BOB's check of K4426's four points, then a targeted re-walk.
- **R-STOP.** A "reading follows" test asserts the words are in `text_units` and the LAST line is searchable by `op=search`, never only that a reading exists.
- **R-CLOCK.** Workerd freezes `Date.now()` in synchronous work: tests assert counts (bytes pulled, calls, rows, yields, objects walked), never a time from the object. A wall time is taken by the test outside the object and only where a bullet says so (K4787, X30 is a cause here). Every bound a test waits on is an injected constant (the scheduler's budget, the scanner call bound: 50 ms in the test), and each bullet that named a wall bar names the count that replaces it or the same-run control it is compared with (rB G2); a real-alarm Miniflare test with a 40 s scanner is the slowest test of the suite and runs alone, five runs (R-NOLOAD).
- **R-PROXY** (K4733): live reads only through the environment's proxy, unchanged; the direct route is refused and never tried.
- **R-QUIET.** The PERFORMANCE re-time runs on a quiet copy; a time taken while a reading tick runs is reported as that.
- **R-NOLOAD** (K4787): every heavy file named below runs alone, five runs, and the time-sensitive set also on one core (`taskset -c 0`); the corpus R set (section 7) runs whole in its own session and container.
- **R-LOG** (K4428): the logging a cause plans is itself tested (the line exists, carries the named fields, is written BEFORE the slow await as well as after) and is a count, an instant or an identifier, never a secret.
- **R-SILENT** (K4571, K4630, K4831 (3)): a new bound is stated to the member in the same change, with the file's size and the limit, and never says "read" of a file that is not; a bound that changes what a member may do is Bob's (K1881) and no job raises or lowers one on its own. The standard's figures (K4628) are the bounds; reaching them is Stage 3 (K4834).
- **R-GATE** (new): an entry that depends on a Stage 0 measurement (GAP-0.1 / GAP-0.2) is marked **GATED** below. Its job starts with the non-gated part. The mechanism (rB G13): the figure is a named entry of `PLATFORM_FIGURES`, exported by `runtime-limits` (section 2c), each with `{value, source}`: `source` is today's ruling (for example "K4616 50 MB") until BOB records the P-M row in `PLATFORM.md`, and in the same commit BOB edits the entry to the row's value and `source: "PLATFORM.md P-M<n>"`; a test pins that no entry is changed without a `source` naming a row, and that an entry still citing its ruling holds that ruling's figure. Code never reads the Markdown. No job guesses a platform figure. The protective figures that BOB cannot supply before the STARTs (the pixels a page, the entries of a zip, the retained objects, a page's decoded content) are proposed constants the job uses with a comment "proposed; BOB records" (rB G8; section 2b), and the job reports what its capped child showed.
- **R-LAYER** (new): a module quotes only a module of a lower layer or lower order (`modules.json`); a lower module reaches a higher one's service only through a callback the composition root injects (the pattern of `owe`, `keepLater`, `pendSha`). The two places GAP's wording crosses this (READ_BOUNDS, the registrar) are ruled (K4835 (1), (2)) and their interfaces are specified in section 2c.
- **R-WORDS** (K3930, K4269; rB G1): every sentence a member reads is a stand-in keyed through `localize`, in the module that says it, and its row is owed to `later-ux.md` (section 5, one HANDOFF to UX-DESIGN); the `words` check fails a job that hard-codes text. The member-facing statements this plan adds are listed in section 5 and each START copies the rows for its job. A words row in `setup-words/rows.mjs` (G135d) is BOB's edit with the regenerate step (section 6), since `setup-words` has no job.
- **Ruled (K4835), carried here as ruled and not as recommended:** (1) `READ_BOUNDS` lives in `runtime-limits` (layer 1); GAP-1.2's owner is runtime-limits; every quoting module takes a `uses` edge to it. (2) GAP-1.4's job row in the filing transaction is written through a registrar callback injected into acquisition, capture and doorbell, never an import upward. (3) S22's test script is `pdf-worker`'s. (4) X22 includes `site-profiles`' `client-rendered.mjs`. (5) An empty-password scan PDF is stated, per GAP section 7.2 (N24), until GAP-3.2. (6) The harness's code is test-support's and plane's tests; plane-1 (test-only) merges first. **K4836** adds one cause, G143.

**Section 2c of the plan, the interfaces you provide or use:**

## 2c. The interfaces providers give (rB D2, D3, D4; rA G2, G6)

Providers merge in `modules.json` order, so a user coded before its provider merges needs the provider's interface in text. These are BOB's details (P17), recorded at adoption and copied into every START that provides or uses one. A user job tests against the stub or a minimal local implementation of the contract named here.

1. **The registrar, `registerJob`.** Wired by the composition root, **plane** (plane-2, job 37), in `plane/store.mjs` beside `capture.keepLater = (a) => pending.keepLater(a)` (`store.mjs:434-438`), as `capture.registerJob = (e) => pending.registerJob(e)`; acquisition, capture and doorbell read it as they read `keepLater` (`typeof cap.registerJob === "function"`, else `opts.registerJob`, else the caller proceeds without a row and says so in its answer, never silently); extraction receives it as `opts.registerJob` and its `owe` is `registerJob` bound to a kind. Signature: `registerJob({kind, sha, session?, cursor?, owed?, carrier?, parts?, detail?, verdict?, priority?}) -> {ok:true, existed:boolean, state} | {ok:false, reason}`; **synchronous**, never throws. `kind` is one of `keep`, `reading`, `ocr`, `subresources`, `unpack`, `fetch` (today's `PENDING_KINDS`, `plane/pending.mjs`) and `scan`, `safe_view`, `grade`, `ingest`; `sha` is the WHOLE digest (64 lowercase hex), except `fetch` and `subresources`, keyed by capture `session` as today (`SESSION_KEYED`). Idempotence key `(sha or session, kind)`; a second registration re-arms, updates `cursor`, `owed`, `detail`, `parts` only when given, never resets the counters; `priority: "raised"` is "Read it". **The transaction rule:** `registerJob` is a synchronous write on the object's own SQL storage, so a caller makes it inside the synchronous function it gives `core.transact(fn)` (`capture/index.mjs:462-464`, `doorbell/index.mjs:149-151`), and the row commits or rolls back with the filing; arming the alarm is a separate async `cap.armJobs()` (`schedulerOf(ctx, env).arm(now)`) after the commit. A caller whose `registerJob` returns `{ok:false}` throws inside its transaction (the filing rolls back) or answers a stated refusal, never swallows it (G98, G126c). **The job row** is plane-2's table, additive to `keeps_pending` (`KEEPS_PENDING_DDL`, `plane/pending.mjs`): the existing columns plus `state` (`queued`, `fetching`, `reading`, `partial`, `refused`, `finished`, `final`), `verdict` (JSON `{ok, size, limit, bound_key}`: the plan step's bound verdict), `cursor` (JSON), `written`, `skipped`, `owed`, `failed` (integers) with `failed_why` (JSON by cause), `last_progress_at`, `rate_per_s`, `carrier` (text: the R2 key or `snapshots/<name>`), `reader_version`, `priority`; per-unit attempt stamps in `job_unit_attempts(sha, kind, unit, tries, stamped_at)`; batch idempotence in `job_batches(sha, kind, batch, landed_at)`. The row is read by control-plane's `op=reading` and `owedwork`, by extraction's `completeness` and by the screens. **Stub:** test-support's `jobRegistrarStub()` implements the contract in memory (`registerJob`, `armJobs`, `rows()`, `armed()`), so jobs 23, 24, 25 and 28 test "a kill between the put and the transaction finds one job row" before plane-2; the same test with the real table is control-plane's seam, section 11 of section 3b.

2. **`READ_BOUNDS` and the ledger** (runtime-limits, job 2; K4835 (1)). Files `bio-plane/src/read-bounds.mjs` and `bio-plane/src/byte-ledger.mjs` (added to runtime-limits' `paths`, section 6). `READ_BOUNDS`: a frozen map `kind -> name -> {standard, alpha, protective, key}` (`key` names the member sentence's `localize` key, which the quoting module owns); `boundOf(kind, name)` returns the ACTIVE figure (the alpha, except the light kinds, which take the standard: K4834); `statement(kind, name, size) -> {size, bound, key}`. A quoting module writes `export const X_MAX = boundOf("pdf", "bytes")` with no numeric literal for a ceiling it keeps. `byteLedger`: `hold(n, tag)`, `release(n, tag)`, `peak()`, `held()` for backing stores; `capFor(N)` is the one cap convention; `PLATFORM_FIGURES` (R-GATE).

3. **`readRange(store, sha, offset, length)`** (acquisition, job 23) `-> Promise<Uint8Array | {ok:false, reason}>`; `sha` is the whole digest only; the capture is read from the whole object when it is held whole, else across the parts named by **`partsOf(sha)`** (provenance, job 21) `-> [{sha256, bytes}] | null`, a function of the whole's bytes and independent of the bundle. Used by extraction, file-safety, content and retrieval in place of resolving parts.

4. **`promote`'s register** (provenance, job 21; builder: screens-intake, job 35). `register` rows are wholes; a multipart document's row carries `parts: [{sha256, path, bytes}]`; a part digest is never a `register.capture_sha`; `homes` is computed over wholes only; `CAPTURE_HELD_BY_ANOTHER_BUNDLE` answers `{capture_sha, kind: "whole", holder}`. **Legacy input** (a part sent as its own register row, the shape until screens-intake's change merges and in any cached page): a register row whose digest is a file's `blobSha` and whose `path` matches `snapshots/<name>.partNNN` (`acquisition/index.mjs:1778`) is read as a part of the whole row named `<name>` and goes to the child table `register_parts`, never to `register`.

5. **`decodeText(bytes, {declared, name, kind})`** (ooxml, job 3) `-> {ok:true, text, encoding, assumed:boolean, replaced:number, replaced_ratio:number, disagree:number[]} | {ok:false, why:"ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED", name}`; UTF-8 (BOM or not), UTF-16 with or without BOM, the declared or detected legacy charset (windows-1252, ISO-8859-x); `assumed` is true when no declaration or BOM decided it; `disagree` lists the window offsets whose detection differs from the file's.

6. **`alarmDouble(opts)`** (test-support, job 1; no plane import) `-> {storage, getAlarm(), setAlarm(t), deleteAlarm(), run(fn), retries}`: `getAlarm()` answers null while a `run` is in flight, a spent alarm stays set until deleted, a failed `run` retries up to 6 times. Used by scheduler's `r8-budget` and `r8-stale` and by plane's harness; scheduler takes `uses: test-support` (section 6).

7. **The reader contract** (reading-pipeline-1, job 27; GAP-1.8): `plan(source) -> {kind, pages|rows, verdict, mode}` and `read(source, cursor, budget) -> {units, next, more, final?, counters:{written, skipped, owed, failed}}` over `source = {size, readRange(offset, length)}`; a whole read is a one-piece range; `next > cursor || final` is asserted by the wrapper (G129).

**Your entries, full text (section 2 of the plan, whole entries; the pieces that are yours are those of your row and of section 3a above, the rest of an entry is context for the jobs that carry it; the walker rows and the corpus recipes are quoted verbatim after the entries):**

### G125 · GAP-1.3 Declared sizes checked before any allocation, on every route (security, whatever the carrier): owner pdf-pixels, image-codecs, image-cover, ooxml, odf-reader, pdf-reader, ocr-worker (each in its own job; the pieces by file are in section 3a); each declared dimension, entry size, decompressed length, object count and nesting is checked against G124 before a byte is allocated; a breach is a stated, deterministic refusal with its figure, never `transient`

Intro. (2) Missed: the tests build well-formed files and a bound is judged after the allocation (scans P: "bound by count, not by work"). (3) Failing-first: each hostile file under a capped child at the real cap, refused stated, peak bounded, the process flat. (4) **L**.

- **G125a · The DCT (decoded), CCITT, JBIG2 (below 2^28) and raw routes allocate from header dimensions before any bound (N12 = S5).** (1) `ccittdecode.mjs:119-122, 218-225` (8,000 bytes declaring 60000 x 60000: 7.3 s, 2,269 MB); `dctdecode.mjs:418, 623-648` (a 330-byte JPEG declaring 60000 x 60000 burns 2.1 s; 30000 x 30000 reaches a 2.7 GB allocation; a valid 8000 x 8000 RGB JPEG at 526 MB); `jbig2decode.mjs:71, 815` (16000 x 16000 with 20 bytes: 4.0 s, +250 MB); raw/Flate `pdfstructure.mjs:177-181, 818` (97 KB for 10000 x 10000 grey: 398 MB; pdf-reader's file, so pdf-reader's job 7 carries the raw route: rA D7); the member's frame check runs after (`member.mjs:116`). The plane counts the kill as `transient` and asks again (`reading-pipeline/index.mjs:620-650`; reading-pipeline-1, job 27). (2) Missed: no test declared a size the data could not fill. (3) Red: SCAN D1-D4 and PDF D10 (an 8 KB CCITT stream declaring 60,000 x 60,000 through the real renderer: `IMAGE_TOO_LARGE` from the header, a 200 `ok:false`, the ledger showing no allocation above the stated figure and one decode call made (counts, not the 1 s of the report: rB G2), flat, not retried). (4) **L**; reds that need the committed renderer run against the source until BOB rebuilds the bundle (section 3, rB D10).
- **G125b · JPX tier-2 object bomb (S6).** (1) `jpxdecode.mjs:641-667` (`addPrecincts`, `decodeTile`) builds precinct objects before any packet byte is checked: about 170 bytes cost 629 MB / 2 GB / 7.7 GB at SIZ 500 / 1000 / 2000; 1500 x 1500 with 1 x 1 precincts dies with an uncatchable V8 abort. (2) Missed: the memory bound counts samples and line buffers only. (3) Red: SCAN D6 (SIZ 500, 1000, 2000 with 2 x 2 precincts and 10 bytes of data; 1500 with 1 x 1) refused before the object table, flat. (4) **L**. Dropped with pdf-worker at GAP-3.3 (parity case then).
- **G125c · image-cover sizes buffers by width alone and keeps a subarray per chunk (S7).** (1) `image-cover/png.mjs:158, 171-173` (rA D1: the file has 233 lines and the draft's 342-366 do not exist; `stride = W * bpp` at `:158`, then `prev`, `cur`, `outPrev`, `out` and `filtered` are each `new Uint8Array(stride)`; the existing `checkPixels(W, H, maxPixels)` at `:150` judges the pixel count against `COVER_MAX_PIXELS` 120,000,000, `image-cover/index.mjs:26`, and not the stride) (70 bytes declaring 30,000,000 x 1 RGBA allocates 572 MB); `strip.mjs:71, 105, 148` (1,000,000 empty IDAT chunks +147 MB / +289 MB; 2,000,000 two-byte JPEG markers +297 MB); reached by a member's marked photo and by an image in an uploaded DOCX (`doc-clean`); `case-carriage`'s try/catch cannot catch an isolate kill. (2) Missed: no hostile counts. (3) Red: SCAN D7 and D8 (30,000,000 x 1; 120,000,000 x 1; 1,000,000 IDAT; 2,000,000 markers; a DOCX holding it; a marked photo in a case): row stride <= 8 MiB and chunk count <= 65,536 judged before the buffers, the isolate lives. (4) **L**.
- **G125d · No ratio check on the office path (X7).** (1) `ARCHIVE_RATIO_MAX` 1,100 only in `listArchive` (`ooxml.mjs:1667`, entries 1533); a 123 KB DOCX with a 120 MiB zero-filled embedding peaks 252 MB; a 150 MiB `presentation.xml` in a 154 KB file 166 MB; embeddings and images are inflated and hashed whole (`docx.mjs:855-860`; `ooxml.mjs:1375`); `streamMember` exists and the office path does not use it; `MEMBER_MAX` 256 MiB is above the object (`ooxml.mjs:269,563`). (2) Missed: `makeZip` builds honest packages. (3) Red: DOCS O6 (a 120 MiB embedding in 123 KB; 250 MiB; a 150 MiB `presentation.xml`; a 100 MiB `.rels`; ratio 1,100 and 1,101; a `document.xml` declaring 2 GB): refused before inflation by name, peak ArrayBuffer within the bound, embeddings streamed with a digest stream, never held. (4) **L**.
- **G125e · No cap on a Flate stream's decompressed size on the native path, reaching file-safety's grade (N4).** (1) `pdfstructure.mjs:162` and `:796-835` (a stream under 128 KiB compressed goes to the native uncapped inflate; `INNER_DECODE_MAX` bounds only the checked path); a 201 KB file inflated 200 MB, +897 MB RSS, 7.4 s, `ok:true` (`red.mjs N4`); it reaches the grade (`file-safety/formats.mjs:91`) up to 20 MiB. (3) Red: PDF A8 and `red.mjs N4` (page 3 of 10 `page_over_bound` with its figures; pages 1-2 and 4-10 read; child alive), and the same file graded by file-safety. Streams inflated streaming with a cap (content: G124d; object stream 16 MiB; ToUnicode CMap 1 MiB; embedded file 64 MiB hashed streaming; proposed, BOB sets). (4) **L**.
- **G125f · `bfrange` expanded with no cap; the codespace expansion is a protective limit (N10, first part).** (1) `pdfstructure.mjs:1667-1760`: `<00000000> <00FFFFFF>` expands 16.7 M codes in 16.0 s and +1.1 GB. A range of at most 65,536 codes expanded, a larger one looked up arithmetically (pdf-envelope C2). (2) Missed: no large-range ToUnicode fixture. (3) Red: PDF B7 and `red.mjs N10` part (a): codes expanded counted and at most 65,536 per range (a count, not a stretch time: rB G2), text exact. (4) **L**. The mixed-codespace and sign-flip parts are G132g.
- **G125g · The OCR member's frame check runs after the render and its bounds are late (S16, bounds half).** (1) `ocr-worker/src/member.mjs:116-126` (a 4000 x 4000 PNG is decoded in 1.8 s then refused; the prepared frame can exceed the 4096 edge: 4096 x 3072 becomes 4159 x 3157, 52.5 MB); 61.3 MB was measured in an engine-only worker before the picture, `prepare` and PNG buffers existed; `OCR_PSM` invalid is accepted silently. (2) Missed: the peak working set (bytes + render + PNG + inflated raw + packed + RGBA + wasm copy + model) was never measured in workerd. (3) Red: SCAN D9 (a frame exactly at the bound and one pixel over through the real renderers; two consecutive frames in one warm isolate) judged from the header before the render; an invalid `OCR_PSM` is refused. (4) **L** for the header check; **P** for the member's memory (a fixture OCR'd on biosmoke8, read back). The reading side is G132k.
- **G125h · Zip entry and ratio checks absent on the reader path: `readContainer` has no entry cap and `readPart` no ratio check (S-21).** (1) `ooxml.mjs:451-511` `readContainer`: its loop `for (let i = 0; i < totalEntries; i++)` (`:488`) has no cap on `totalEntries`, and `readPart` (`:593-615`, `locatePart` `:555-566` judging only `MEMBER_MAX` and `ARCHIVE_TOTAL_MAX`) has no ratio check; the only caps, `ARCHIVE_ENTRIES_MAX` (`:265`, checked at `:1533`) and `ARCHIVE_RATIO_MAX` (`:272`, checked at `:1667`), sit in `listArchive` (`:1478`) (rA D2: the draft called `:1533` `readContainer`) (60,000 entries read; an honest 19.4 MiB part from 58 KB reads in 1.5 s). Office: 100,000 entries / 16 MiB directory; archives 10,000 (docs B3). (2) Missed: the limits lived in `listArchive` only. (3) Red: SHEET X15, X16 and DOCS O8 (60,000 entries; a lying central directory) refused by name. (4) **L**.

### G126 · GAP-1.4 The READ JOB table and one registrar (S1): owner plane (`keeps_pending` becomes the job table for reading kinds; plane-2, job 37; the registrar is the callback of section 2c, wired by plane in `plane/store.mjs` and called by acquisition, capture, doorbell and extraction); prov acquisition, capture, doorbell, control-plane, reading-pipeline (store-door only if its first record finds a change: its 413 text and `STREAMED_BODY_OPS`, `store-door/dispatch.mjs:373`). **GATED** for `limits.cpu_ms` (S-35, P-M5/P-M6 via GAP-0.2): starts with the table, the registrar and the gate; `cpu_ms` is set in `bio-plane/wrangler.jsonc` to the figure `PLATFORM.md` records, and until then stays unset with a comment

Intro. Every way in writes one job row keyed by whole sha and kind, in the filing transaction, before the answer, through a callback injected by the composition root (K4835 (2); R-LAYER; the callback and the row are specified in section 2c, rB D2); registration is idempotent; "Read it" raises priority; no member request does more than a first look (head and tail ranges, declared size); a plan step refuses above either limit with its figure. (2) Missed: E6 and scan30 were fixed on the path the test used (scans P: five recurrences of one class, "OCR stops at 24 pages" four times); gap fixed by testing every way in (table in `OPTIONS.md` 2.1). (3) Failing-first: each way in with a kill between the put and the transaction finds one job row and no inline read; the providers below the table (acquisition, capture, doorbell, extraction: jobs 23, 24, 25, 28) count rows on test-support's `jobRegistrarStub` (section 2c), and control-plane's seam repeats each with the real table after plane-2 (section 3b, section 11). (4) **L**.

- **G126a · A reading that leaves OCR pages unread owes them to nobody at the source (G96 = N2 = S1 = F-2).** (1) `Extraction.read` passes no `owe` (`extraction/index.mjs:683-692`); the only registration is the promote hop (`control-plane/index.mjs:854, 2681, 3869`), which the walker's browser cancelled after 497 ms (`diagnosis-R8-live-reads.md` A2); `readHeld` answers `more` before computing `owed` (`extraction/index.mjs:849-853`); `readUploaded` looks at `reading_pending`/`more` only (`control-plane/index.mjs:2756`); code `reading-pipeline/index.mjs:1552-1554`. Row `A.md:21` (copied verbatim from round 8 G96). The registrar is extraction's `owe`, which is the injected `registerJob` of section 2c with `kind: "ocr"`, called before the answer and by the continuation pieces; control-plane writes no second row (logging only); registered only when `ocr_left` pages remain. (2) Missed: `t51-round5-seam.test.mjs` E6's scan is 18 KB and its OCR stub answers 24 pages in one call, so the real loop never runs; `r37.test.mjs` tests `owe` with a hand-written hook. (3) Red: seam "R9 (G96): a 30-page 1.9 MB scan" with an OCR binding of one page per call at 1,000 ms: before any promote `op=owedwork&sha=` lists an `ocr` entry (red: `owed: []`); within the ticks all 30 pages are in `text_units`, `completeness` whole, `ocr_left` absent; a promote cancelled 0.5 s in leaves the row standing; SCAN C1/C3/C4 and PDF D4 (100 text then 30 scanned pages in pieces: pages 124-129 get units); unit `extraction/r8-owe.test.mjs` (`owe` called once with `{kind:"ocr", sha, cursor, pages_left}` when pages remain, never when none). (4) **L** and **P** (a scan uploaded and its promote abandoned by navigating away; `owedwork&sha=` lists the row). **Old records (R-OLD):** the walker's scan (`0d3f6804...`) and the ODS (`84a1a376...`) hold readings with `tier3_candidate` or `ocr_left` and no row; a migration in `migrateRepairs` (`extraction/index.mjs:1982`, extraction's, K4811 (2)) re-registers them, counted at P.
- **G126b · The inline reading pass has no OCR bound: 24 OCR calls in series inside `captureupload`, 42.7 s for a 1.9 MB scan (G97).** (1) `tooBigToReadInline` defers a PDF above 2 MiB only (`control-plane/index.mjs:733-747`, `READING_DEFER_PDF_BYTES` :213, clock half :2667-2672); `askMemberPerPage` (`reading-pipeline/index.mjs:357-401`) makes a first call unconditionally (:362) then up to 24 (:354, :372); `ocrInvocations` cannot express 0 (:2611). Row `A.md:21`. Ruled (K4811 (6)): the inline pass makes no OCR call, a 3-page scan included; `extraction.read` (extraction-1, job 28) and `acquireReadingOp` pass `ocrInvocations: 0` for the member's request; the other inline paths with a member call in a loop (tier 2 and tier 4) are bounded the same way, and the discovery is a test, not the job's search (rB G12): a counting-door walk (`wrap-entry.mjs`) over every op that can run a reading inside a member's request (`captureupload`, `acquire`'s reading op, `extractread`, `readheld`, `pdfstructure&ocr=1`, `pagetranscribe`, the promote follow) with an OCR binding that counts calls and fails any call made inside the request: zero calls, an owed row instead. (2) Missed: E6's OCR stub is instant, no test took the wall of `captureupload`. Fix: the row is registered BEFORE the inline read starts; a fresh stub (`env.STORE.get(id)`) on a throw, one retry, then a stated refusal; `reading_pending` follows whether the row is held. (2) Missed: every ledger test stubs `pendingadd` as succeeding. (3) Red: the plane's seam section G98 with the `STORE` binding wrapped so the first `stub.fetch` rejects and the stub STAYS broken (`plane/shape/harness.mjs` `fault: {remote: true}`): the row is written through a fresh stub; where it cannot be, the answer does not say `reading_pending` and carries a stated refusal; `extractread` made to die mid-read leaves a row. (3) Red: seam "R9 (B2): a 1 MB ODS that unpacks to 20 MB" (about 1.03 MB stored, `content.xml` 20,400,000 B deflating 20:1): no `extractread` during the upload, `reading_pending: true`, a `reading` row before any promote; unit `control-plane/r8-gate.test.mjs` (defers by declared unpacked size; a small ODS is still read inline; a lying directory defers); audit: one fixture per container kind (xlsx, docx, pptx, odt, zip/epub), small stored and over-bound unpacked, each deferred; the heap-capped node child proves the old path dies at 112 MB. - **G131a · UTF-8 CSV cells are lost through the cp1252 transport (S-4).** (1) `csv-reader/index.mjs:508, 751`: Latin-1 supplement 26 %, Cyrillic 39 %, curly quotes and dashes 45 %, CJK 62 %, emoji 100 % lost when the file claims `utf-8`; a 6.9 MiB file with `’` in 300,000 rows gives 300,000 undetermined cells. The carrier uses a bijective byte map, not `TextDecoder("iso-8859-1")`. (2) Missed: ASCII fixtures. (3) Red: SHEET C27 (UTF-8 with `’`, `É`, `Ñ`, CJK, an emoji, 300,000 rows): no valid character null (J-17). (4) **L**; hypothesis for workerd is run first.

A quote opens quote mode only at the start of a field; an unterminated quote at a window end is stated with row and size. (2) Missed: no stray-quote rule stated. (3) Red: SHEET C14 (a stray `12" pipe` at row 100 of 500,000: all rows read). (4) **L**.
- **G131c · The dialect fails all or nothing (S-6).** (1) `csv-reader/index.mjs:285-295, 340-362, 372-417`: one quoted newline, ragged row, title line or CR-only ending in the first 50 lines gives one column; UTF-16 without BOM reads as us-ascii with NULs; a UTF-8 file whose first MiB is ASCII is `us-ascii, certain`; a first line over 1 MiB gives "too few lines". Decide per window with stated disagreements, up to 1,000 lines or 1 MiB by majority. (2) Missed: one dialect per fixture. (3) Red: SHEET C15, C18, C19, C26, C29 (windows of 4,099, 40,000, 50,000, 70,001 bytes; ragged with a 5-line title; CR-only; non-ASCII after 1 MiB; UTF-16 no BOM). (4) **L**.
- **G131d · An empty cell shifts the row's values left; empty cells keep their column (S-3 (b)).** Owners csv-reader (job 4, the text path) and calculations (job 31, the table seam; a real change, so sonnet: rB G4). (1) `P100,,100` gives `["P100","100"]` with only an "empty" count (csv-reader text path; table seam `calculations/index.mjs:506-515`); also G128h stores empties. (2) Missed: the calculations fixture hands text whole (P2). (3) Red: SHEET C17 through `tabledeclare` and search. (4) **N**.
- **G131e · `cellsIn` is quadratic: CSV re-walks from byte 0, XLSX inflates and CRC-checks the whole sheet part per call (S-20); point reads must answer within 2 s from precomputed rows (GAP-1.8 (d)).** Owners csv-reader (job 4) for the CSV half and office-readers (job 5) for the XLSX half (rB G3; `formats-xlsx.mjs` is office-readers' file). (3) Red: counts, not time: bytes served per `cellsIn` at row 700,000 is bounded by the window, not the offset. (4) **L**.

The light kinds are read in byte windows with a carry of the unfinished field, line or tag; the table rows `text`, `csv`, `tsv`, `json`, `xml` carry the standard (50 MB, 1,048,576 rows) as their active figure; a file above is refused with its size and the limit. ### G133 · GAP-1.8 (c), office and ODF readers made correct within the alpha: owner office-readers (docx, pptx, xlsx), odf-reader and ooxml own their bullets; prov doc-clean (G136), reading-pipeline

Intro. ODS gets held cells and row windows and merged ODS cells keep their columns; office text readers stop throwing on an out-of-range character reference and have work bounds against quadratic scans; the ZIP central directory is read once in physical order with an entries limit; classification is a budgeted step. (2) Missed: no office file was ever run through the plane's harness; the builders make well-formed UTF-8 text-heavy files (docs P1, P4). (3) Failing-first: DOCS and SHEET corpus files through upload and the ticks. (4) **L**.

- **G133a · An ODS cannot be read within the bound: odf-reader has no held-cells contract, so a 20.4 MB `content.xml` dies at the object's heap and over 20 MiB is refused (G99 = S-2; S-1 for ODS).** (1) `odf.mjs` has no `inlineCellsMax`/`cellsIn`/`cells_held` (`odf.mjs:2202` ignores `inlineCellsMax`; `odf.mjs:674-700, 679-690`, R35/R48); 172,302 cells, a 29.7 MB reading, a 142 MB heap uncapped, dying at 112 MB (`diagnosis-R8-reading.md` B (1)(a)). Row `A.md:37`. Fix: the held-cells contract of csv-reader R17/R18 (`text(parts, {inlineCellsMax, yieldNow})`, `cells: null` and `cells_held: true` past the opt-in, `cellsIn(parts, {sheet, fromRow, toRow, ...})`), `content.xml` read as a stream of rows in windows; the 20 MiB guard STAYS (K4831 (3), R-SILENT; rA D9): the job does not raise it, and if the capped child shows the held-cells path reads more, the figure change is BOB's to report to Bob (K1881), not the job's. (2) Missed: `odf-reader/large.test.mjs` tests the over-bound refusal; `g41-held.test.mjs` was xlsx and CSV only. (3) Red: `odf-reader/r8-held-cells.test.mjs` in a child at `--max-old-space-size=112` on a 20,400,000 B `content.xml` (`sheets[0].cells === null`, `cells_held`, the whole sheet text, peak under the bound; `cellsIn` rows 70000-70010 equal the whole listing's); door "R9 (B, plane 2b): a 19.5 MiB ODS": `op=reading` whole, the LAST row in `text_units`, `op=cells` rows 1..50; SHEET O03-O06 (a file over the stated bound, 20 MiB, is refused with its figure; the capped child's finding is reported, the bound unchanged). (4) **L** and **P** (the ODS digest's `owedwork`, `op=reading` raw, `heldcaptures`, `captureupload` Server-Timing). Old records: stored ODS readings stated "too large" are re-read by an extraction migration (`too_large.key == cells`, container ODF), counted at P.
- **G133b · ODS `table:covered-table-cell` is never iterated, so every cell right of a merge shifts one column left; wrong citations (S-18).** (1) `odf.mjs:1507` (B ran: a cell at C1 reads as B1). XLSX merged ranges are unread and unstated (a stated gap, parity case in Stage 3). (2) Missed: no merged cell fixture. (3) Red: SHEET O09 (merged cells with values to the right keep their columns), X14 stated. (4) **L**.
- **G133c · "Never throws" is false: `&#x110000;` in `core.xml`, a rels target or `[Content_Types].xml` throws `RangeError`; a lone-surrogate rels target throws `URIError`; a deterministic throw is `transient` and retried for ever (X12 = S-21 first part).** (1) `ooxml.mjs:722-730` (no range test, unlike `docx.mjs:177`, `pptx.mjs:181`); `subresources.mjs:810` (`encodeURIComponent`); the pipeline records the throw as `FAILURE_TRANSIENT` (`reading-pipeline/index.mjs:2720-2724`); `meeting-calendar.mjs:151` (`Math.min(...dates)` throws at 150,000 dates, doctypes). A throw on the file's own bytes is `final` with its reason. (2) Missed: no hostile character reference. (3) Red: DOCS O7 and SHEET X17 (`&#x110000;`, `&#99999999999;`, `&#xD800;`, `&#0;`, `&#12ab;` in `core.xml`, a rels target, `[Content_Types].xml` and the body): never throws, deterministic failure `final` with its reason. (4) **L**.
- **G133d · Quadratic scans on tiny input: a 953-byte DOCX takes 8.0 s, a 400 KB unclosed `<w:comment ` 20 s, ODF `<a ` 9.7 s, `doc-clean/xml.mjs:130` 21 s (X6).** (1) `docx.mjs:196, 425, 803-805` (`TOKEN_RE`); `pptx.mjs:197`; `odf.mjs:359, 1195-1212, 1954-2013`; `doc-clean/xml.mjs:130` (G136d). A linear scanner (no backtracking attribute group), a tag at most 64 KiB, the walk counts bytes scanned per tick; yields every 64 Ki tokens (the office entries have none: `entry.text` takes one parameter). (2) Missed: the object cannot time itself (R-CLOCK); no work count. (3) Red: counts of bytes scanned at most 4 times the part for DOCS O7/P6/D4 (unterminated tag, 40,000 copies of `<a b="`, 20,000 unclosed comments, `<!x>` x 200,000). (4) **L**. Dropped at Stage 3.
- **G133e · Archive listing is unbounded and repeated: a shuffled central directory of 10,000 entries costs 9,180 part reads and 106 s, physical order 25 reads (X4).** (1) `ooxml.mjs:1537, 1563-1572, 1604`; `acquisition/unpack.mjs:125-158, 259-272, 578-606` (entry rows keep neither method, CRC nor offsets, so each 100-entry tick lists again: 306 evidence operations per 100 small entries); 2,000 entries with 60,000-byte names list at 343 MiB. Parse the directory once in physical order; directory at most 16 MiB, a name at most 4,096 bytes. (2) Missed: central directories in file order only. (3) Red: DOCS A5, A6, A4 (10,000 entries shuffled: part reads at most 2 x the physical-order count, ticks do not list again; 10,001 refused whole by name). (4) **L**.
- **G133f · `doctypeFor` classification is unbounded in one piece: 6.9 s at 12 MB, 14-16.6 s at 24 MiB, a 12 MB DOCX/HTML classified whole through `readText` (X25; X22's quadratic patterns are G140).** (1) `reading-pipeline/index.mjs:1918, 2418-2447, 2662`; `docprofile/doctypes/registry.mjs:85-112`; `readtext.mjs:263-265`; a 1 MB CSV makes 129 MB of garbage; `op=reading` of a finished 1,328-unit PDF reading kills the object. Classification is a budgeted step: head-and-sample above 2 MiB for every kind, each detect given a window and a work count. (2) Missed: yields only between detects and only for plain text (G88). (3) Red: DOCS T1/T4 and Q1 by count: classification work per tick bounded, the tick's longest stretch within the bound. (4) **L**.

(3) Red: DOCS S1, SCAN F-family: encrypted is `encrypted`, never `clean`; a RAR not openable is `unknown`, stated. (4) **N**.
- **G136b · The scanner's render output is unbounded and file-safety holds it whole (X17); a failed safe-view render is permanent (X19).** (1) `file-safety/index.mjs:438, 946, 982` (`new Uint8Array(await res.arrayBuffer())`, then `bucket.put` twice); `file-scanner/.../renderer.mjs:120-143` converts everything then cuts to 500 pages; a 192 KB DOCX of 1,875 pages renders to 186 MB; `clamav.mjs:93-109` stages every target (up to 200 x 256 MiB) before one `clamscan` (S13 (g)); X19: `index.mjs:976-981, 1009-1061, 392-398, 692, 715` (only `NOT_FOUND` is requeued; `threatOf` keeps every member's bytes until the call returns; `#due()` reads every `fs_files` row). The derived PDF is read as a stream with a 32 MiB limit, cut at the last page that fits (`x-truncated`); the page cap applies before conversion; a failed render is classed (`TIME_LIMIT`, `SCANNER_UNREACHABLE` retried with back-off; `NOT_RENDERABLE` final); one render per tick with an in-flight mark. (2) Missed: instant scanner; clock never advanced 10 days. (3) Red: DOCS S2, S4, S5 (a 21 MiB DOCX; the 1,875-page DOCX; a 362 KB DOCX expanding to 155 MB; the renderer healthy 10 days after `TIME_LIMIT`, clock advanced; a 256 MiB archive of 5,000 members: `threatOf` peak within the bound). (4) **L**; **P** for the live render of a small DOCX.
- **G136c · doc-clean writes a 16-bit entry count (no ZIP64, no entry bound), and a cleaner crash loops (X21).** (1) `doc-clean/package.mjs:55-60, 83-123`: a valid 70,009-entry DOCX cleans to `ok:true` with an end record saying 4,473, which `ooxml.readContainer` also lists (it never checks it reached the directory's end); `case-carriage/index.mjs:1270-1282`; `tried` stamped after the work returns. ZIP64 or a stated `TOO_MANY_ENTRIES`, the written count equal to the read count, `tried` stamped before. (2) Missed: the oracles (Info-ZIP, Python) were not applied to the cleaned copy. (3) Red: DOCS C1 (70,009 and 65,535 entries; a crash mid-clean; the next wake does not loop). (4) **L**.
- **G136d · file-safety grades and classifies images wrongly (S13 a-f).** (1) `file-safety/index.mjs:415-417, 736-738` (every image over 1 MiB graded `high`; R6 says low by source); `:1569` (TIFF/HEIC/BMP deeper check `incomplete` for ever); `formats.mjs:65` (`%PDF-` within the first KiB runs before `isImage`, G130d); `index.mjs:1568` (structure check answers `clean "image"` without opening); `:462-468` (`unknown LIMIT:ScanTime` loses its reason). Images graded by source, not size; TIFF/HEIC/BMP named "unread image type"; the structure check says `not_examined`, never `clean`; one signature table. (2) Missed: no 1 MiB + 1 JPEG. (3) Red: SCAN A16 (1 MiB, 1 MiB + 1, 3 MB, 25 MB JPEGs all graded low), D13, F1-F8. (4) **L**.
- **G136e · Reading does not wait for the scan, said plainly; a hold gates nothing (S13 (i), K4831 (6) (a)).** (1) No reading module imports file-safety (the open/refuse gate is `file-safety/index.mjs:805-813`); decoders run before the daily scan. The document page states the policy; a found threat's text leaving search is GAP-3.9, not this round (P19: the scanner has no signatures, FR-SCAN). (2) Missed: the policy was nowhere stated. (3) Red: a screen test that the Document page carries the sentence; a held document is still read (SCAN E10). (4) **N**.

### G137 · GAP-1.12 The smaller screen answers (K4831 (10)): owner per bullet

- **G137a · The safe view keeps 500 pages and says so (DOCS Q3; file-safety, words).** (1) `file-scanner` page cap 500 at 150 dpi, time 600 s (`renderer.mjs:125`); the safe view is a view, not a reading, and is said to be (docs A6, E3). (2) Missed: the cap was a constant, not a stated sentence. (3) Red: the safe view of a 600-page document says "the first 500 pages" with the figure (DOCS S2). (4) **L**.
- **G137b · A spreadsheet preview's "show more" pages by `next_row` (SHEET B-6, E-26).** (1) `file-safety/formats.mjs:112-116` (1,000 cells a sheet, 20 sheets, 5,000 cells, ODS 8 MiB declared). The screen is a HANDOFF to UX-DESIGN (section 5); file-safety provides the paging by `next_row`. (2) Missed: no preview of a 100,000-row sheet. (3) Red: a preview of SHEET X03 pages through `next_row` to the last row by count of calls. (4) **L**. Waits on the design screen for the draw; the op is built now.
- **G137c · "As a visitor saw it" is said exactly, and a failed visitor render is charged its real time, filed as the document, and retried by class (X23, DOCS Q7).** (1) `capture-sources` `browserrender.mjs:450, 566, 631-698` and `render.mjs:172, 404, 442-504`; `acquisition/index.mjs:1485, 1503-1511, 1813-1827, 2192, 2282-2286`; `capture/index.mjs:2351-2364`: a failed render returns no `elapsed_ms`, so the whole 25 s reservation stays charged to the 1,200 s daily allowance (about 48 failures switch visitor views off until 00:00 UTC, and the refusal says "held by renders running right now"); `RENDER_FAILED` is `final` for every cause; the content test is "html non-empty" (a 403, a `chrome-error://` page is accepted). Charge the real time (plus close) and show the day's account; reject a status >= 400, a `chrome-error://` location or an empty shell, stated "the page did not render"; retry class (`transient`: session refused, 3 per second, open bound, up to 3 times with back-off; `permanent` final with its detail); the copy's words: first load, not scrolled, banners not dismissed, no frames or shadow content. GAP-1.12 (c) names the charge and the words; the content test and the retry class are the same cause X23, planned here, while scroll, shadow DOM and the 25 MiB DOM bound stay stated limits (section 2b). (2) Missed: the only tested page is 60 bytes; the fake browser cannot show scroll or banners. (3) Red: DOCS V4-V7 (offline loopback origin, real Chromium for fidelity): a failed render charged its elapsed time; a 403 and a `chrome-error://` page not filed as the document; the 12 s page delivered via the owed path. (4) **L**; **P** (acadia's visitor view with `timings.stages`).
- **G137d · Illegible pages: turned upright first; under 1/5 legible no text is stored and the page is stated; under 4/5 the text is kept and marked partly legible, for photos and PDF pages alike (S17's asymmetry, B-S6).** (1) `ocr-worker/src/member.mjs:184` (a picture is checked, a PDF page accepted at any quality; R25 and its control test say so); `OCR_CONFIDENCE_FLOOR` null everywhere; legibility stored beside the text. (2) Missed: no sideways or faint PDF page. (3) Red: SCAN B3, B8, B9 (inverted, blank, handwritten, faint) as pictures and as PDF pages: the same rule. (4) **L**.
- **G137e · Analysis workbooks are held whole, with only the cells a binding or citation touches interpreted, up to 250,000 cells; `addWorkbook` stops throwing on a cell with no reference and counts what it drops (SHEET B-4, E-22, S-26, S-32).** (1) `workbooks/index.mjs:42-44, 280-287` (50,000 cells; `<c>` without `r` throws; unreadable and duplicate references dropped with `ok: true` and no count; `ENGINE_MEASURE` literals :51-57); sheet-worker's values (`contract.mjs:33-39`: 9,000,000 B, 250,000 cells, 120,000 ms) are in no requirement (requirements only, section 2b). (2) Missed: the engine's bound was never compared with the workbook's. (3) Red: SHEET X20 (50,000, 130,000, 250,001 cells: add/bind/recompute; the first two accepted, the third refused with its count), X12 (rows with no `r`). (4) **L**.

**Corpus recipes, verbatim from the family reports (size, shape and the check of each named case; the first word names the family).** Reading the tables: PDF and SCAN rows are `# | File (how made) | Path | Passes when | Owner`, DOCS rows `ID | File, how generated | Path | Passes when | Owner`, SHEET rows `Id | The file | Today | Judged by`; `R` is the real path through the plane's harness, `M` the owner's interface, `L` the live subset, sizes are the file's bytes unless "declared". A case is built by the generators of test-support (`pdfgen`, `imggen`, `sheetgen`, `docgen`) with the family's fixed seed unless the row names one; the smallest case that shows the cause runs in your job (a scaled window or an injected bound, R-FOUR (3)) and the full size in the corpus R set at the round end (plan section 7); a generator option not yet in the merged library is built in your own test directory first, marked `// MOVE` (G123 intro). The J-n and I-n and SI-n invariants a row cites are quoted after the rows.

**Invariants of the PDF family (verbatim):**

### 2.4 The invariants (the "always", stated once; each corpus file is judged against all of them)

The invariants each name the module whose requirements carry them; BOB writes the wording into those requirements (§4).
- **I1 Every page once.** Each page in `[0, pages)` is read exactly once across the pieces, in order, and the units equal the generator's truth for that page.
  - Requirements: reading-pipeline R40, pdf-reader R43.
- **I2 Progress.** Every tick that answers `more: true` moves the cursor forward; the number of ticks is at most `pages` plus 2.
  - Requirements: reading-pipeline, plane R77.
- **I3 Never silent.** Every page that is not read whole carries a named reason in the reading and in the member's statement. `completeness` is "whole" only when the cursor is at the end, nothing is owed, and no page is unread for a reason other than "has no text".
  - Requirements: extraction R91, K4571.
- **I4 Memory.** The child running the real plane at 128 MB, with the held bytes inside it, stays alive, and its peak is within E4's budget.
  - Requirements: pdf-reader R41, reading-pipeline R36, superseded by E4.
- **I5 Work per tick.** Every tick's counted work is within E1's budget, and its longest synchronous stretch is within E2's (a count of operators between yields, not a time).
  - Requirements: reading-pipeline R40, plane R93.
- **I6 Pieces equal whole.** The stored reading of a PDF read in pieces equals its whole read, field for field, the fields of F2.
  - Requirements: reading-pipeline R40, R12.
- **I7 A refusal is stated, final and finite.** Every bound in §2.2 answers its named statement once. Nothing is owed forever, no kill is retried more than E6's 3 times, and no row stays "being read" after the last tick.
  - Requirements: plane R77, extraction R82.
- **I8 State loss is harmless.** Losing the open document, or restarting the object, between any two ticks gives the same reading and units; only the tick's work differs.
  - Requirement: reading-pipeline R40.
- **I9 Every path owes what it leaves.** Upload, promote, press, ledger, `op=pdfstructure` and `pagetranscribe` all owe pages and ranges a cap left.
  - Requirements: reading-pipeline R37, extraction.
- **I10 Search finds the last line.** The last line of the last page (and a first and a middle line) is found by `op=search` and is citable.
  - Requirements: extraction R22, R90.
- **I11 The reading pages to its end.** `op=reading` with `next` reaches the last page with no answer over 1 MiB; the Document screen's Next page reaches it.
  - Requirements: extraction R93, screens-inquiry.
- **I12 Stored values bounded.** No stored value exceeds 1 MiB, and none exceeds the platform's value bound.
  - Requirement: extraction R96.
- **I13 Contention.** While the corpus file's ticks run, cheap reads through the door wait under 500 ms at p95.
  - Requirement: plane R93.
- **I14 Same bytes, same answer.** Two runs of the same file give byte-identical readings and units.
  - Requirements: pdf-reader R24, reading-pipeline.

**Invariants of the SCAN family (verbatim):**

### 4.4 The invariants (the "always", stated once; each corpus file is judged against all of them)

The invariants name the module whose requirements carry them; BOB writes the wording into those requirements (§6). PDF #1's I1-I14 hold for scanned PDFs; these are the scan family's, with the ones it shares noted.
- **SI1 Every page once, and what is left is owed.** Every page of a scan is OCR'd exactly once or stated unread with a named reason; any page a cap leaves is owed on **every** path (upload, promote, press, ledger, `pagetranscribe`, `pdfstructure`); `ocr_left` is read by every caller. *(reading-pipeline R37, extraction R82; PDF #1's I9.)*
- **SI2 Never silent.** `completeness` is "whole" only when nothing is owed and no page is unread for a reason other than "has no text"; a type not read is named; a refusal's own reason reaches the member (S2, S3, S11). *(extraction R91, K4571.)*
- **SI3 Declared before allocated.** Every route judges the header's declared size before any allocation or inflate; a hostile header gets a named 200 refusal in under 1 s and the process stays flat (S5-S7). *(image-codecs, pdf-pixels, image-cover.)*
- **SI4 Memory.** The plane child at 128 MB with the held bytes inside it stays alive and within PDF #1's E4 budget; the OCR member's peak working set (C1) stays within its measured bound for every admitted frame. *(pdf-pixels, ocr-worker, plane.)*
- **SI5 Work.** No member request does OCR or a decode over its K4354 bar; every tick's OCR wait is within C3. *(control-plane, reading-pipeline, plane R93.)*
- **SI6 Progress and finiteness.** Every `ocr` tick that answers `owed` advances or is `final` and stated; a kill is an attempt; a page killed 3 times is stated; no row is "being read" after the last tick. *(plane R77, ocr-worker.)*
- **SI7 Search finds the last line.** A marker word on the last line of the last OCR'd page (and a first and a middle one) is found by `op=search` and is citable with its page. *(extraction R22, R90.)*
- **SI8 Truth.** A page that OCR reads contributes its words at the stated floor (≥ 90 percent on a clean page; the amended floor on a stated-hard one); a page below the legibility floor is stated and is not stored as text (S9, B-S6). *(ocr-worker R25.)*
- **SI9 State loss is harmless.** Restarting the object between any two ticks, or losing the open document, gives the same reading and units. *(reading-pipeline R40; PDF #1's I8.)*
- **SI10 Pieces equal whole.** The OCR text of a scan read in pieces equals the whole read's, and each piece's chain names its pages (S25). *(reading-pipeline R40.)*
- **SI11 Stored values bounded.** No stored value exceeds 1 MiB, and none exceeds the platform's value bound. *(extraction R96.)*
- **SI12 Same bytes, same answer.** Two runs of the same image give the same pixels (`pixels_sha256`) and the same units. *(pdf-pixels R40, image-codecs R8.)*
- **SI13 Honest acts.** No act is offered on a document that it cannot perform; a type not read says so by name once. *(screens-inquiry, extraction.)*
- **SI14 Contention.** While a scan's ticks run, cheap reads through the door wait under 500 ms at p95. *(plane R93; PDF #1's I13.)*

---

**Invariants of the DOCS family (verbatim):**

### 4.4 The invariants (the "always", stated once; each corpus file is judged against all of them)

I1-I14 are the PDF plan's (every unit once; progress; never silent; memory; work per tick; pieces equal whole; a refusal is stated, final and finite; state loss is harmless; every path owes what it leaves; search finds the last line; the reading pages to its end; stored values bounded; contention; same bytes, same answer), read for *units* (paragraphs, slides, lines, members) instead of pages. The kinds here add:
- **I15 Declared is not allocated.** A bound is on what the read allocates and walks (objects, buffers, tokens), with ArrayBuffers counted; a file whose declared size passes and whose walk would not fit is refused *before* the walk, by name. Requirements: office-readers R12, ooxml R35 (rewritten), reading-pipeline R36.
- **I16 An encoding is named.** Every text read states the encoding it was decoded in; a decode that replaces more than the tolerance is partial with the count; nothing is `U+FFFD` and `failure: null`. Requirements: reading-pipeline R1, R32; docprofile R18.
- **I17 Every part is read or named.** A part of a file that is text is a unit; a part not read is named by kind and count in the reading and in the member's statement; the units of a document are never lost to a bound on something else (cells, links, comments). Requirements: office-readers R11; extraction R91, R97; K4571.
- **I18 Every member is accounted for.** An archive's members are each `filed`, `read`, `refused with a cause`, or `waiting for a named act`; none is `waiting` for ever; an uploaded archive is opened like a fetched one. Requirements: acquisition R38/R40.
- **I19 A copy says what it is.** A visitor view carries its fidelity ("first load, not scrolled"), a failed one carries its detail and its retry class, and a copy that did not render is not filed as the document. Requirements: capture-sources, acquisition R100.
- **I20 A deterministic failure is final, with its reason.** A throw on the file's own bytes, or a refusal by a bound, is never `transient`; a transient failure is retried under R77 until it ends or becomes final with its reason. Requirements: reading-pipeline R37; plane R77.
- **I21 A verdict that was not reached says so.** A file the scanner could not look inside (encrypted, over a limit, unrarred) is never `clean`; the member is told which. Requirements: file-scanner R3; file-safety R51.
- **I22 The statement names the cause.** The sentence for a refusal is the one for its cause, with the figure; "Read it" is offered only where it can succeed. Requirements: reading-pipeline R37; screens-inquiry; setup-words.

Corpus files are judged against I1-I22. Lines in §5 say which are particular to the file.

---

**Invariants of the SHEET family (verbatim):**

### 4.4 The invariants (the "always"; stated once; each corpus file is judged against all of them)

The PDF plan's I1-I14, re-cut for a sheet. Each is a sentence for the module named; **a counted bound, never a time** (R-CLOCK). The corpus and §5.3 refer to them as J-n.

- **J-1 Every row once.** Each data row of the file is in exactly one indexed unit; the header line is the *first line of a block* and never a data row; every unit's extent contains exactly the rows it holds. (reading-pipeline R41, new text)
- **J-2 Progress.** A read finishes in at most ⌈unit text ÷ 2 MiB⌉ + 2 ticks; no tick ends with the cursor unmoved; a failure of any tick is retried, never ends the read as finished. (reading-pipeline R40; plane R77)
- **J-3 Never silent.** `completeness` is `whole` only when every row of the file is in the index; anything cut, held, skipped, undecodable or refused is stated with its **size, its bound and its cause in the cause's own words** (not "undecodable region"). (reading-pipeline R15/R35; extraction R90/R91; K4571, K4630)
- **J-4 Memory.** A reader's retained heap between windows is at most 2× its window plus O(columns); the whole read stays alive in a child at the platform's cap with the held bytes inside it. One cap convention for every format: **the platform's measured idle, written once** (S-38).
- **J-5 Work per tick, by count.** A later tick hands an entry at most 5 MiB; it hands the store at most 5 MiB of ranged reads; every window is followed by a yield; no stored value exceeds 1 MiB. (reading-pipeline R40, csv-reader R20, office R35, odf R-new)
- **J-6 Pieces equal whole.** The cells and the text of a windowed read equal a whole read's, and both equal the generator's truth, field for field. (The oracle is the generator, never the reader's past output: P7.)
- **J-7 A refusal is stated, final and finite.** Past a bound the answer is final, names the size and the bound, and no row remains "being read" after the last tick.
- **J-8 State loss is harmless.** A restart or a store failure between ticks (after any tick) neither loses a row nor ends the read: the read resumes or re-reads, once.
- **J-9 Every path owes what it leaves.** Upload (one part and multipart), the doorbell pull, address `acquire`, the daemon paths, and the no-cursor door read all end `whole` or owed; none ends `whole` with `truncated`.
- **J-10 Search finds the first, a middle and the last row,** by a mark placed in a non-header cell; the hit's extent contains the row; `cellsOf` of that extent contains the cell with the mark equal to the generator's.
- **J-11 Cells keep their place.** The cell at A1-style address *x* of a file is *x*, with empty cells, merged cells (the covered cells keep their columns), rows without `r`, and a styled empty cell not moving a value or the used range; text and cell views agree.
- **J-12 Stored values bounded.** No stored value and no reading row over 1 MiB; **a reading is never replaced by a statement while its text could be indexed** (the ODS cliff); a reading over the guard still has its units.
- **J-13 One table, one sha.** A table declared from the same data, whichever kind (CSV comma or semicolon or tab, XLSX, ODS), whichever path (sheet range, document extent, passage, bytes), whichever window cut, has the same rows, the same columns and the same sha; the header appears once; an empty cell keeps its column; a quote, a tab or a line break in a value is a value.
- **J-14 Money by truth.** An ingest of a generated table writes the facts the truth names, each fact's source row is the sheet row; a percent, a currency mark, a locale separator and a date are read as the table declares them, or stated.
- **J-15 Same data, same answer, in every kind.** A workbook and its CSV, TSV and ODS twin give the same cells and the same searchable words, dates included.
- **J-16 Contention.** While sheet ticks run, twelve cheap reads wait under 500 ms at p95 on workerd (R93), **asserted** for the real reads, not printed.
- **J-17 Encodings.** Every cell whose characters the file's encoding can represent is read: no valid UTF-8 character is `null`; a windows-1252 file is decoded with the encoding stated; an undecodable cell is counted, listed up to 200, and never shifts a column.
